PowerShell is now used only to read the PowerShell commands Claude runs, with PowerShell's own parser, where PowerShell exists; elsewhere Claude uses Bash, read by Codehouse's own parser. A port to another system supplies its own `Protector` and pickers (the Keychain and native windows on a Mac).
- **Chat build CH-02, the data model (ADR 0013 storage, ADR 0015 branching):** `apps/host/src/chat/` holds Chat's own store in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Gatehouse\chat\chat.db` through Node's built-in `node:sqlite` with FTS5 (no dependency, no native addon; FTS5 checked on the shipped Node 24.15.0). `migrations.ts` numbers additive migrations, each in its own transaction; an older Codehouse reads a newer file. `db.ts` (`ChatStore`): conversations with provenance (native, imported from claude.ai, fork), messages with parent links, provider and model, attachments, artifacts and versions, memory entries (imported ones inactive) and import batches, all redacted before they are written, with a count of redactions per message. Imported conversations are read only. Search quotes each word so nothing you type is FTS5 syntax, and covers text blocks only. `tree.ts`: the shown branch with every sibling at each fork, and switching to a sibling follows the newest answer below it. Nothing is wired to the window yet: CH-03 runs native chats through it.
- **Chat build CH-03, first part: chats recorded, and versions you can move between (ADR 0015):** `ChatRecorder` (`apps/host/src/chat/recorder.ts`) writes every native chat into Chat's store as it happens: your message when you send it (a Host-only `prompted` event, which reaches no screen and no phone), Claude's reply when the turn ends (text, thinking, tool calls and up to 2,000 characters of each tool's answer), each with the SDK session it belongs to (migration 2) and the reply's last SDK message id. A Retry or Edit (`create_session` now carries `replaces`) is a sibling at the reply the fork resumes from, or a new first message in the same conversation; a chat brought back after a restart, or a branch you switch to, carries on where its SDK session ended. Coding sessions, plugin evaluations, a subagent's own steps and replayed history are not recorded. The admin API serves the branch (`/api/chat/branch`), switches it (`/api/chat/switch`, which answers with the SDK session the branch ends in, so the screen resumes it in the pane), lists conversations and searches them. In the window, a chat message with more than one version shows ‹ 2 of 3 › before Copy, Retry and Edit (`BranchNav`); your messages on screen are matched to the stored branch in order, and the arrows are left out when the two disagree. Still to come in CH-03: the outputs folder and its Approver rules, the conversation sidebar from the store, artifact views and memory shown as injected.
- **Chat build CH-03, second part: a chat's tool posture (ADR 0012):** rules may now be scoped to a kind of session (`sessionKinds: [chat]` or `[code]`; `sessionKind` in the evaluation context, sent by the Host's canUseTool and floor hook for every call, accepted by the Approver's evaluate tool). The deny rule `chat-no-shell` refuses Bash, PowerShell, BashOutput, KillShell, Write, Edit, MultiEdit and NotebookEdit in a chat, as a second wall behind a chat's tool list, which has none of them; each refusal is audited with its rule id. A call that does not say counts as a coding session. Deviation: no `outputs` folder yet, so a chat writes nowhere, which is within the prompt's posture and stricter than it: chat folders are outside the workspace roots, and writes to one folder per chat need roots scoped to a session first. Queued as its own item.
- **Chat build CH-03, third part: finding chats:** Ctrl+K searches what was said in your chats (In your chats, from two letters, a moment after you stop typing, through `/api/chat/search` over Chat's store). Picking one opens it where its shown branch ends (`/api/chat/open`: that message's SDK session and the folder it ran in, kept from migration 3), or goes to it when it is already open. Chats in no project are grouped by when they were started, as in Claude's desktop app: Today, Yesterday, Previous 7 days, Previous 30 days, Older.
- **Chat build CH-03, fourth part: documents and code as artifacts (ADR 0019):** `save_artifact` also takes `markdown` (a letter, a plan, notes) and `code`. They are kept, versioned, redacted and audited as pages and pictures are, and shown as text in the Artifacts pane (the app's Markdown view, which renders no HTML written inside, and its code view), never in a frame, with Copy and Download (`.md`, `.txt`). A document is marked as made by AI in a comment Markdown does not show; code by its headers only. Fixed on the way: an export's file name used the kind as its extension; it now uses the stored file's own.
- **While you were away, written in plain English (owner, 4 Nov: "These messages dont tell me anything"):** when you come back after ten minutes or more, the card asks the Host (`/api/recap`, `apps/host/src/recap-api.ts`) and Haiku writes three or four sentences: what was done, what is still left and anything waiting on you (`recap-writer.ts`). The list of turns, files and decisions stays underneath, and is the whole card when Claude does not write it: turned off (Settings, Phone and notifications, `recapByClaude`, absent is on), a chat on a model on this computer (never sent), an account that has not passed the organisation check, or a failure. The window sends only your messages and Claude's own words since you left, not tool output or a subagent's words; the Host redacts each line, keeps the latest 12,000 characters, asks once per absence, and audits each recap by session and size, never its words. The query has no tools, runs in Manual mode with a canUseTool that refuses everything (not `dontAsk`, invariant 3), with no project settings, in the temp folder and with a cleaned environment. Note: invariant 15 limits the policy judge, which never sees transcripts; this is a separate feature the owner asked for, which reads only what was said, and it decides nothing.
- **Chat build CH-03, fifth part: the memory a chat was given, as given:** each chat keeps the memory entries it was told when it started (already redacted, the same list the `memory_used` audit entry names by id), and its Memory side panel shows them in those words, with where each came from (every chat, or the project), through `/api/memory/given`. It is asked for only when the panel opens. CH-03 is complete except the outputs folder, deferred by ADR 0012.
- **Chat build CH-04, first part: the importer (ADR 0014):** `apps/host/src/chat/import/` reads a claude.ai export into Chat's store. `zip.ts` checks the whole zip before reading anything (ZIP64 too) and streams each entry through its size and CRC; `json-stream.ts` reads `conversations.json` one conversation at a time (1 GB in about 6 seconds within 512 MB, run with `CODEH_BIG_IMPORT=1`); `map.ts` maps each record as the owner's report observed it, keeping unknown fields raw and unknown blocks whole, rebuilding the tree (the root sentinel, several roots, a lost parent) and the artifacts' versions; `writer.ts` writes it all in one transaction, redacted, skipping records already imported unless the export's is newer; imported project instructions and memory stay inactive until you accept each one (`ChatStore.pendingImports`, `accept`, `dismissMemory`). Migration 4 finds an imported conversation by its claude.ai id. Test fixtures build synthetic exports in all three layouts and every zip attack (`apps/host/test/fixtures/`); no real export data. Still to come: the import screen, the admin route with its audit entry, and the feature flag.
- **Chat build CH-04, second part: importing from the desktop:** `/api/chat/import` opens the Windows open window for one zip (nothing in the request names a file), imports it and writes one audit entry with the zip's SHA-256, its layout and its counts; a refused zip says why and that nothing was imported. `/api/chat/import/pending`, `accept` and `dismiss` list and decide each imported project's instructions and memory entry, each decision audited. Codehouse-Helper.exe gained `--open-files` and `--open-file` (title and file types in environment variables), so attaching files no longer needs PowerShell either; PowerShell stays only as the fallback.
- **Chat build CH-04 complete, and CH-05, the imported chat viewer:** Settings, Privacy, Chats from claude.ai says how to get the export (and what a workspace owner must turn on), imports it, says what came in or why nothing did (with the files the zip holds), and lists each project's instructions and memory entry to accept or turn down. Imported chats are listed under From claude.ai on the Chat side of the sidebar and open in a read-only viewer (`ImportedChat`) drawn by the native chat's own parts (`toItems` turns stored blocks into the same items: words, folded thinking, tool calls with results, cloud steps labelled Cloud, files the export left out, unknown parts folded and never run, and the redactions per message), under the banner "Imported from claude.ai on {date}. Read-only local copy.", with ‹ 2 of 3 › on any message with other versions, its artifacts as text, and Export as Markdown. Ctrl+K lists hits In your chats and From claude.ai apart (the source filter). No feature flag: CH-04 shipped once the import screen and the viewer were both ready, so imported chats are never listed without a way to open them. Still to come: Open in Claude (CH-06) and Continue locally (CH-07).
- **Chat build CH-06, Open in Claude (ADR 0017):** an imported chat's Open in Claude (`/api/chat/handoff`) opens `https://claude.ai/chat/{its claude.ai id}` in an Edge app window: msedge.exe from App Paths, exactly `--app=<url>` and `--profile-directory=<profile>`, detached, nothing read back. Settings, Privacy, Open in Claude chooses Edge (the default), the default browser, or the Claude app's `claude://` link (off unless turned on, and Edge when Windows does not know it), and the Edge profile (Default unless you choose another). The browser and the app open through Codehouse-Helper.exe `--open-url`, which accepts only https and claude://. Every hand-off is audited with its target and address.
- **Limit protection off for a while (owner, 5 Nov: "an option to disable limit protect per session and choose a time period ... After the time period end they are automatically re-enabled"):** a session's menu has Limit protection off for 1, 3, 8 or 24 hours (the paused banner offers the same beside Start now), and Turn limit protection back on. `Session.setLimitProtection` (`set_limit_protection`, at most a day) sets the session's `protectionOffUntil`, which the limit pause already skips (commit e255c59, the plan limit and the spending cap both), starts a session a limit had paused, and audits each change (`limit-protection`, decided by you). The session's info carries `protectionOffUntil`, shown as "Limit protection off until 14:30" under the session with Turn back on. Nothing needs waking at the end: the pause compares against the time at each look, so protection is on again by itself.
- **Chat build CH-07, Continue locally (ADR 0016):** an imported chat's Continue locally opens Trust and continue (`ContinueDialog`), which shows exactly what the new chat will be given (`/api/chat/continue/preview`: the shown branch, each turn redacted again, as one marked history document; its size and a token estimate; the project's instructions and imported memory only if ticked, both off), and, past the budget (`chatContinue`: 10 turns kept, 60,000 characters), a summary of the earlier part by a separate one-turn call with no tools (`summarise.ts`), shown first. Trust and continue (`/api/chat/continue`) starts the chat with exactly that preview, once, through the organisation guard in Plan mode; the recorder files it as a fork of the imported chat (`forkOf`), the chat says "Continued in Codehouse. Not synced to claude.ai.", and the fork, the choices and the seed's size are audited.
- **Chat build CH-08, desktop features (the Chat build is complete):** the quick prompt also carries on in the chat you used last; it takes a picture of part of the screen (drag a rectangle over the frozen screen, Esc cancels) or of the window you were in (`packaging/Capture.cs`, in Codehouse.exe; the quick prompt steps aside while it is taken) and shows it with "Pictures are not redacted"; sending puts it in the chat's folder through `/api/files/attach-data` with `source: "screen-capture"`, audited as `screen_capture`. Notifications for a long reply and for a request that needs you already come from the notification kinds (4 Nov) and cover chats. MCP in chats: only the in-process Companion, since the managed policy blocks other servers (ADR 0012). `.mcpb` desktop extensions are planned, not built (`docs/plan/mcpb-extensions.md`). Deviation: voice is out of scope, as the prompt says.
- **/btw side questions (owner, 4 Nov, for a colleague: "I use them a bit to ask for progress reports or to just do some troubleshooting while it carries on"):** `/btw <question>` in a session's prompt box goes to `POST /api/btw` (`apps/host/src/btw.ts`) with what you and Claude said in it (no tool calls, no subagents' words), never to the session, which carries on. The Host redacts the question and the lines (invariant 12), refuses for a model on this computer and for an account that has not passed the organisation check (invariant 14), audits `side_question` by size only (rule `side-question`, invariant 13), and asks a separate one-turn query as the recap does: no tools, Manual mode with a canUseTool that refuses everything, no project settings, the temp folder, credentials stripped (invariant 6), the session's words marked as data, its latest 30,000 characters. The answer shows in a By the way card above the prompt box until Got it and is not added to the conversation. Claude Code's own /btw (`askSideQuestion`) is in `sdk.mjs` but not in `sdk.d.ts`, so it is not used (`docs/sdk-notes.md`).
- **Settings overhaul, output style:** Settings, Sessions, Output style (`outputStyle` in Gatehouse settings): Default, Claude Code's Explanatory or Learning, or one of your own by name. A coding session starts with `Options.settings: { outputStyle }`; a chat is not changed. Inline settings can carry permission rules, so the safety check (renamed `isSafeInlineSettings`) allows this one key only with a plain name (`OUTPUT_STYLE_NAME`: letters, numbers, spaces, dots, dashes, underscores, up to 60), beside the two that turn things off; the settings schema holds it to the same pattern, and the page leaves out a name it cannot use. Sessions already open keep theirs.
- **Settings overhaul, worktree location:** Settings, Sessions, Worktree location (`worktreeLocation` in Gatehouse settings, read each time a worktree is made): beside the project (the default, `<repo>.worktrees/<name>`, not written), inside the project (`<repo>/.codehouse/worktrees/<name>`, with `/.codehouse/worktrees/` added once to the repository's own `info/exclude` through `git rev-parse --git-path`, never its `.gitignore`), or a folder you choose (`<folder>/<repo name>/<name>`). A chosen folder must be inside a workspace root in effect, because `outside-workspace-write` would deny every write a session there made; outside them the worktree is refused with a reason and nothing is made. Older versions ignore the key and put worktrees beside the project.
- **Settings overhaul, pull requests:** Settings, Sessions, Pull requests (`pullRequests` in Gatehouse settings). Branch prefix: new worktree branches are `<prefix><date>-<hex>` (absent or unusable: `codehouse/`; `BRANCH_PREFIX` refuses options, `..`, `//` and `.lock`, and git checks the rest). Open a pull request (off by default), as a draft (on by default) and fix failing checks: a session in a worktree, and only there, is told in its instructions to commit, push its own branch (`git push -u origin <branch>`) and open the pull request with `gh pr create`, never to push another branch or force, and to read `gh pr checks`. Codehouse itself never pushes or calls GitHub: each push and gh command is Claude's, through the floor and the Approver like any other (`git-remote`, risk 80, asks you), except that Extreme approval approves escalations by itself, as it does for every other one; the page and the help say so. Auto-archive after a merge is left out: it would need Codehouse to ask GitHub with your gh sign-in.
- **Settings overhaul, browser settings, first part (Viewer):** Claude desktop's Browser settings mapped to what Codehouse has: its viewer shows only pages on this computer, so Settings, Sessions, Viewer has two choices kept in the window (`lib/viewerPrefs.ts`, like Appearance): open a page or picture Claude writes (a successful Write of `.html`, `.htm`, `.png`, `.jpg`, `.gif`, `.webp` or `.svg`; only ones written after the pane began showing that session, so history and another session's files never open), and open a link in Claude's words to localhost or 127.0.0.1 in the viewer (`TranscriptLink`; any other link goes where it always went). Both on unless turned off. Keep cookies and Allowed sites have nothing to act on (the viewer loads only local pages, sandboxed). Claude using a browser itself (Browser tools) is the next part, with its own safety design.
- **The items waiting on an owner decision, decided 9 Nov under the owner's standing rule (choose the best option, safety first, and record it):** F13 and G39 are left out: a self-test of the preview with console errors, and click-to-edit or "Fix this error" from the preview, need either browser automation (waiting on IT) or a debugging protocol (banned on this machine), and the preview is another origin that Codehouse cannot reach into; page looks (headless Edge, localhost only) stay as they are. G33 is to be built with CodeMirror, already used for the read-only views, not Monaco, which would be a new dependency: your own edits, saved through the Host only inside the session's folder, never to a file the floor blocks, refused when the file changed outside since it was opened, and recorded. G36 follows G33, read-only parts first (history and blame) and anything that changes the repository shown before it is done. G32 and O18 follow the owner's decision of 30 Oct (sessions on the phone, end to end encrypted, each prompt and Interrupt signed with the passkey and recorded); starting a new task from the phone is limited to templates you set up on the desktop (B35's safe variant), so the phone never sends a folder or free instructions to start in.
- **O21, the session details panel, 9 Nov (owner, with two screenshots: "I like this pop out menu ... It even shows info on running background tasks ... and how long those tasks have been running for"):** a **Details** panel in each pane's side rail, for sessions and chats (`components/SessionDetails.tsx`): Environment (computer, folder, repository and branch, worktree, model, spend), Context (share used, used of the most, and each part with its tokens and share, opening and closing), Connectors (each MCP server's name, state and kind: in Codehouse, a claude.ai connector, or outside), Changes (lines added and removed since the first checkpoint, with Open Changes) and Working in the background (each task with how long it has run, counting every second). The Host gives the computer, repository, branch and connectors (`session_details`, `session-details.ts`: names, states and kinds only, never a server's address, command or settings) and now keeps when each background task began (`backgroundTasks` in the session's state, kept when Claude Code sends the list again; `background` stays for older readers). Everything is written out, with no hover hints. Tests: session-details.test.ts (connectors without their settings, the repository and branch), background.test.ts (start times kept across list changes).
- **Fix, limits in Settings always hold; a bypass per session, 9 Nov (owner: "I had the limit set to 85% and the app kept going past it anyway"; then "ensure that if a limit is set in the settings for the percentage use and extra spend that the platform honors this unless a user specifically bypasses it for a specific session ... a timer ... or a maximum additional spend ... one or both. Once either of the limits is reached the session stops again until the user configures another bypass"):** the cause was the 5 Nov change (e255c59) that let extra usage override the pause point: with extra usage on and some left, the plan limit counted as 0%, so on this account (extra usage on) nothing paused at 85%. Now:
  - The plan's pause point and the most spending a day pause every session whatever extra usage is left (`LimitPauser`, `fiveHour` only).
  - The only way past them is a bypass for one session (`Session.setBypass`, `set_limit_bypass`, desktop only): a time (1 minute to 24 hours), a most it may spend (up to $1,000), or both. While it lasts neither limit pauses that session. The pauser ends it when its time passes or its spending reaches the most (the session's reported spending since it began, or the account's extra usage since then, whichever is more, so it never stops late), and pauses the session in the same look if a limit is still over. The session looks at the limits before Extreme approval carries on, and at the moment its time runs out, so it does not run past by up to a minute.
  - Removed: "Start now" holding off further pauses until the reset (5 Nov), a message lifting a pause, and "Limit protection off" with no spending limit (its command, `set_limit_protection`, now sets a bypass with that time). Start now works only once no limit is over, and says why otherwise; a message to a paused session is refused with the way to bypass.
  - A bypass is recorded before it applies, and refused if it cannot be; its end is recorded with why and what was spent. It is not kept across a restart, so after one the limits apply again.
  - Tests: limit-pause.test.ts and limit-pause-session.test.ts (the 85% case with extra usage left, time, spending by the session and by extra usage, Extreme stopping at the spending limit, a message not lifting a pause, Start now holding nothing, a bypass needing a limit and refused unrecorded). Break checks: extra usage overriding the pause point, the time ignored, the spending ignored, a message lifting the pause, no look before carrying on, an unrecorded bypass applying; each fails its test.
- **Frontier D-040, the intent drift alarm, 12 Nov:** in a session with a goal, at the end of a turn (at most every five minutes), a model on this computer compares the goal with what Claude said it is doing in that turn's reply, and answers on track or drift with a reason (`apps/host/src/intent-drift.ts`). Drift is recorded as `intent_drift`, shown at the top of the session (Drifting from the goal?), and your phone is told; a later check on track clears it. Only a runtime on this computer (Ollama or an OpenAI-compatible server at 127.0.0.1, Settings, Models, with other models switched on) is used, never a gateway, so nothing leaves the computer; both texts are redacted first, and the agent's words are given as a report to judge, never as instructions. With no such model the feature says it is unavailable and nothing is asked. **Deviation:** the check runs at the end of turns rather than at each checkpoint, which in practice are the same moments, so it needs no hook in the checkpoints. Tests: intent-drift.test.ts (a divergent report raises the alarm and one on track does not; what is sent is redacted; no local model, or a gateway, is unavailable and asks nothing).
- **Frontier D-048, the architecture review pack, 12 Nov:** **Review pack (arc42)** in the Architecture view saves an arc42 document for the project in Markdown, with all twelve sections, each filled from what Codehouse already reads (`apps/host/src/arc42.ts`, the `arc42` command): goals from the requirements (D-033), constraints and crosscutting concepts from the architecture rules (D-041), context and building blocks from the C4 diagrams (D-042), the solution strategy and decisions from the ADRs (D-038), quality requirements from the fitness checks (D-045), the deployment view from the deployment files it finds, and risks from drift, failing checks and threat drafts. A section with no source is marked as a gap, saying what would fill it, and the save says how many. Tests: arc42.test.ts (every section present, the filled ones from their sources, and the five with none marked as gaps).
- **Frontier D-046, the public API break guard, 12 Nov:** in a library (a package.json that is not private and names main, module, types or exports), an edit that removes an exported name from a TypeScript or JavaScript module (a function, class, constant, interface, type, enum, a name in an export list, or the default export) goes to a person as `api-break`, with each removed declaration shown, never approved by Extreme approval and asked in Accept edits and Auto too (`apps/host/src/api-surface.ts`). Adding an export, or changing what one does, is not a break; test files and an application's modules (a private package) are left alone. **Deviation:** exports are read with Tree-sitter, since the TypeScript language service this would use (C-032) is not built yet; it reads the same declarations a .d.ts would hold. A .NET project with ApiCompat is detected and the reason says to run it; Codehouse never installs or runs it itself. Tests: api-surface.test.ts (a module's exports; removing an exported function escalates in Extreme approval with its declaration shown; adding one and changing a body do not; an application is left alone).
- **Frontier D-039, requirement change impact, 12 Nov:** when a change edits a requirement in docs/requirements (D-033), Changes lists what it is linked to: the code it names and the files its commits changed, its tests, and the ADRs that mention its id (`impactOf` in `apps/host/src/requirements.ts`, the `requirement_impact` command), so you can see what may need to change with it. Tests: requirements.test.ts (an edited requirement lists its code, tests and ADRs).
- **Frontier D-038 and D-044, architecture decision records, 12 Nov:** **Decisions...** in a session's menu drafts each decision the session made (a question you answered, with its options and why the chosen one; a plan you approved) as an ADR in MADR format, with front matter naming the session and its audit record, numbered after the project's existing ones (`apps/host/src/adr.ts`, `adr_drafts`). You can edit a draft; **Accept and write** has the Host write it into `docs/adr/NNNN-name.md` (nothing else, and never over a file that is there), recorded as `adr_written`. A question's answers are now kept in its `question_answered` record (redacted like any record), so the choice can be drafted. **D-044:** the project's accepted ADRs (not proposed, superseded, deprecated or rejected) are added to each coding session's instructions, those whose `paths` match first, at most 4,000 characters; an ADR's front matter can say what it rules out (`forbids`: words, packages or calls, optionally only under `paths`), and an edit that adds one goes to a person as `adr-contradiction`, naming the ADR, never approved by Extreme approval, and asked in Accept edits and Auto too. Tests: adr.test.ts (a decision produces a draft linking to the session; it is written only into docs/adr, and not twice; the accepted ADRs are in the context and a superseded one is not; a contradicting edit is flagged with the ADR named, and one outside its paths is not).
- **Frontier D-050, the threat model delta, 12 Nov:** when a change adds an HTTP route (Express, Fastify and the like, Flask and FastAPI, ASP.NET), a data store (a database pool or client, Prisma, Mongo, Redis, SQLite, a DbContext), an outbound connection (fetch to an address, axios, http requests, WebSocket, Python requests, HttpClient), a listener (listen, createServer, a WebSocket server, uvicorn) or a new dependency (D-056), Changes lists it under the threat model, with **Copy the drafts**: a STRIDE table for each, with the question to answer for spoofing, tampering, repudiation, information disclosure, denial of service and elevation of privilege (`apps/host/src/threat-delta.ts`, the `threat_delta` command). Read from the lines the change adds; commented lines are left out; nothing runs. Tests: threat-delta.test.ts (a new HTTP route drafts an entry with all six STRIDE questions, as do a data store, an outbound connection, a listener, a Flask route and a new dependency; a commented route does not).
- **Frontier D-045, fitness functions, 12 Nov:** a project can set checks with limits in `.codehouse/fitness.yml` (`apps/host/src/fitness.ts`): `maxFileLines` (the longest a source file may be), `maxFanOut` (how many other modules one module may import, from D-041's graph), `maxDependencies` (packages package.json names) and `forbiddenApis` (regular expressions, optionally only in some files, with a reason). They are a gate: when a run of Extreme approval says its goal is met or its queue is empty while one fails, it is not done; it stops as `fitness`, recorded as `fitness_gate_failed`, and your phone is told which checks fail. The change's evidence pack holds the results, with a note when one fails, and the Architecture view lists them. A file that does not parse checks nothing, and says why. Tests: fitness.test.ts (each check fails past its limit and passes within it; no settings check nothing, unparsable ones say so; a goal run is told it is not done).
- **Frontier D-042 and D-043, living C4 diagrams and drift alerts, 12 Nov:** **Architecture...** in a session's menu draws the project from D-041's import graph (`apps/host/src/arch-view.ts`, the `architecture` command): modules are folders (two deep, such as src/domain), and the C4 context (the project and the packages it uses), container (its layers, or its top folders without rules) and component (its modules and their imports) diagrams are given in Mermaid, to copy into documentation. **Approve as it is** keeps the module graph as approved (`.codehouse/arch/approved.json`, recorded as `architecture_approved`); after that, drift is shown first, as an alert: a new module (also marked new, in red, in the component diagram), a new edge between modules of different layers, and a new import cycle between modules (strongly connected sets of two or more). **Deviation:** the diagrams are shown as Mermaid text with Copy rather than drawn, so as not to add a large drawing library; GitHub and most documentation tools draw them. Tests: arch-view.test.ts (the three diagrams, a new module marked as drift and nothing marked before it; a new cycle and a new crossing raised; nothing is drift before anything is approved).
- **Frontier D-041, architecture rules as policy, 12 Nov:** a project can say how it is built in `.codehouse/architecture.yml`: `layers` (each a list of file globs), `allow` (which layers each may depend on; a layer not listed may depend on any) and `forbid` (imports by module name, optionally only from some files, with a reason) (`apps/host/src/architecture.ts`). An edit (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) that adds an import breaking one goes to a person as `architecture`, naming the rule and why, and Extreme approval never approves it; the floor hook asks in Accept edits and Auto, where an allowed edit would not reach canUseTool. The file is worked out as the edit would leave it, and only imports it does not already have count, so a file that already broke a rule is not stopped for every other change. Imports are read with Tree-sitter (TypeScript, TSX, JavaScript, Python: import and export from, require, dynamic import, Python's import and from), so an import in a comment or a string does not count; relative imports are resolved to the project's files. A rules file that does not parse, or names no layers, checks nothing and says why. The project's import graph is kept in `.codehouse/arch/graph.json` and read again only for files whose size or time changed, for D-042, D-043 and D-045. Golden cases: `apps/host/test/golden/architecture.yaml` (11). Tests: architecture.test.ts (the golden cases; a Domain-to-Infrastructure import escalates in Extreme approval with the rule named; malformed rules check nothing; the graph is not written again when nothing changed).
- **Frontier D-036, tests-first contract mode, 12 Nov:** **Tests first...** in a session's menu takes the acceptance criteria (the session's goal by default) and sends Claude a task to write tests for them first. Until you approve them, an edit to anything but a test file goes to a person as `tests-first`, which Extreme approval never approves; a banner says so, with **Approve the tests**, which locks (D-007) the test files the session changed since its first checkpoint, ends the mode and tells Claude to implement without changing them. Recorded as `tests_first_started` and `tests_first_approved`. Requirements (D-033) can be pasted in as the criteria. Tests: tests-first.test.ts (an implementation edit asks in Extreme approval while a test file does not; approving locks the tests and ends the mode, and approving nothing is refused).
- **Frontier D-037, the assumption register, 12 Nov:** a coding session's in-process Companion offers `declare_assumption` (text, and optionally how to check it): Claude records an assumption it is making instead of quietly guessing (`apps/companion/src/handlers.ts`, the session's `declareAssumption`). Each is kept by the session, recorded as `assumption_declared`, shown on the progress card with **It holds** (recorded as `assumption_verified` by you), and goes into the change's evidence pack (D-003) until verified. The policy rule `assumption-tools` allows it only with `mcpSource: [sdk]` in a code session, so a tool of the same name from any other server, or from a server that is not Codehouse's own, still asks. Tests: assumptions.test.ts (allowed from the in-process Companion only; the same name from stdio, http or no source does not get through; what is declared is kept, an empty one refused, and an unverified one is in the pack).
- **Frontier D-033, D-034 and D-035, what was asked for, 12 Nov:** **Requirements...** in a session's menu reads the project's own `docsequirements` folder: one Markdown file per requirement, with front matter (`id`, `title`, and optionally `tests`, `files` and `status`) and its words, EARS wording welcome but not needed (`apps/host/src/requirements.ts`, the `requirements` command). Files are read tolerantly: one without front matter, or with front matter that is not valid, takes its id from its name or first heading and is listed under Read with care, as is a second file with an id already used. Each requirement is linked to the commits whose message names its id, the test files that name it or that it lists, and the evidence packs of those commits (D-003, whose packs are made per commit); a requirement no test names, or a link to a file that is not there, is listed as a gap, first. **D-034:** Changes lists each change (hunk) that neither the session's approved plan (from the ExitPlanMode call in the audit log) nor any requirement accounts for, with the reason: its file is named by neither, and nothing it adds (functions, classes, routes) is mentioned. **D-035:** the progress card says how many files the session changed that the approved plan did not name (the `scope` command). Without a plan and requirements, nothing is flagged. Tests: requirements.test.ts (requirements read tolerantly with their problems; links to commits, tests and evidence; the one with no test, and a link that does not resolve, are gaps; an extra feature is flagged and a planned one is not; files outside the plan counted).
- **Frontier D-028, the causal provenance graph, 12 Nov:** **Where it came from** beside each file in Changes says, for each of its changes (hunks), which edit call made it and what the session had read before that call (files, pages, other servers' results, newest first), marking what D-061 found holding prompt injection and what is not the project's own words (pages, other servers, D-015's untrusted files) (`apps/host/src/provenance-graph.ts`, the `provenance_graph` command). A hunk is tied to the newest edit of its file whose written text holds the hunk's added lines, or else the newest edit of the file. It is built from the audit log alone; D-061's `injection_flagged` record now names the call it came from (`toolUseId`), so the flagged page is the one marked. Tests: provenance-graph.test.ts (a hunk shows the injected page and the README read before the edit that made it).
- **Frontier D-031, evidence queries, 12 Nov:** the Audit page has **Saved questions**: who approved what (allowed by you, your phone or a push answer), every deny for a project, every network call (WebFetch, WebSearch, and shell commands that download, send or name an address) and every Extreme approval (`apps/host/src/audit-index.ts`, `GET /api/audit/query`). They are answered from an index kept beside the log (`%LOCALAPPDATA%Gatehouseaudit-index`), plain JSONL, one file per month, with only what the questions need; before each question it reads only what each month file gained (it remembers each one's length, and starts a file again if it was replaced), and only whole lines, so a record being written is read next time. No database and nothing native. Results are newest first, up to 200 by default, and redacted again before they are given. Tests: audit-index.test.ts (each question answers what it says, redacted; a later record found without indexing the rest again; 100,000 records answered in under 2 s for each question).
- **Frontier D-066 and D-006, characterisation capture and the behaviour diff, 12 Nov:** Changes lists the changed JavaScript and TypeScript files that nothing in the project's tests names, with **Capture behaviour** and **Compare with now** (`apps/host/src/characterise.ts`, the `characterise` and `behaviour_diff` commands). A capture takes the file as it was at the checkpoint before the change, finds its exported functions with Tree-sitter, calls each with generated inputs (small, ordinary and edge values, up to 60 cases) from a harness Codehouse writes, and records what each gave back or threw; it runs twice and keeps only what gave the same answer both times, so the kept cases replay green, and it is redacted before it is kept (`%LOCALAPPDATA%Gatehousecharacterisation`). Compare with now replays the same inputs against the file as it is and says each output that changed in plain English ("price(42, \"a\") returned null before the change, and now returned 0."). The harness runs as a Codehouse action in a sandbox copy of the project, never in your working tree; recorded as `behaviour_captured` and `behaviour_compared`. **How it is offered:** rather than stopping an agent before its edit, the capture is made afterwards from the checkpoint's copy of the file, which is what the file was before the agent touched it; the effect is the same and nothing waits. Tests: characterise.test.ts (the exported functions and that nothing tests the file; a capture redacted and replayed green; one intentional change shows exactly one behaviour delta, in words; the project untouched).
- **Frontier D-029b, audit anchors signed on the phone, 12 Nov:** the phone app's Sessions has **Audit log, Anchor with Face ID**: the phone signs an envelope of the new type `anchor`, naming the chain's head as the computer last shared it (`auditHead` in the sessions snapshot), with its passkey, like a decision (invariant 8: the pinned passkey, a fresh nonce, a short life; an anchor naming anything else is refused). The Host checks that the record is in the log with that hash and keeps the anchor (`anchor-<seq>-p.json`, signer phone, strength passkey) with the passkey's WebAuthn assertion, and the phone's public key in `anchorsphones.json`; recorded as `anchor_signed` by your phone. Both verifiers check it without the Host: the client data is a WebAuthn get over the envelope's challenge (the sha256 of its canonical JSON), user verification was done, the envelope names the anchored record, and the passkey's signature holds; the PowerShell one turns the DER signature into the form Windows PowerShell 5.1 checks. The daily anchor on this computer goes on as before, since a Face ID prompt each day would be one more prompt; the phone's is the stronger one, made when you choose. Both verifiers now ask for this computer's key only when one of its own anchors is there. Tests: phone-anchors.test.ts (a passkey-signed anchor accepted, one naming a session refused, kept and recorded, passed by both verifiers; a record the phone did not see with that hash refused; a changed envelope fails both).
- **Frontier D-004 and D-005, mutation testing of a change's new lines, 12 Nov:** **Test the tests** in Changes makes small changes to the lines the change added (=== and !==, < and >=, + and -, && and ||, true and false, a ! taken away), one at a time, and runs the project's tests on each (`apps/host/src/mutation.ts`, the `mutation_run` command): a change the tests still pass with has survived. It reports how many were caught, the score, each survivor with its file, line and before and after, and, when Settings has one for the project (`mutation.thresholds`, by folder name, read tolerantly), whether the score reaches it. The mutants are made in a sandbox copy of the project (git's tracked and new files) with its dependency folders linked, never in your working tree; the sandbox is removed link first, so nothing it points to is touched. Each test run is a Codehouse action (judged by your rules, no credential, inside the session's job, stopped by the kill switch, recorded), the tests must pass before any mutant counts, a run that reaches the memory cap stops and says so, and the run is recorded as `mutation_run`. **Deviation:** StrykerJS and Stryker.NET are detected and named, but the run uses Codehouse's own mutator for JavaScript and TypeScript, which needs nothing installed and no network; running Stryker itself (which installs packages and mutates more than the changed lines) is left for when a project wants it. **D-005:** each survivor has **Write a test that catches it**, which sends the session a task naming the change; while the task runs, an edit to a file that is not a test file goes to a person as `test-scope` (never approved by Extreme approval), and when its turn ends the mutant is run again (`mutant_rechecked`, killed or survived). Tests: mutation.test.ts (a weak test leaves a survivor, test files are never mutated, the project and its linked dependencies untouched; the memory cap stops a run with its reason; an edit outside the test files asks in Extreme approval while a test file does not; the killed count rises once a test catches the mutant).
- **Frontier D-027, step-exact reconstruction, 12 Nov:** **Rebuild here** on a call in a session's Timeline rebuilds the files as they were at that step in a folder of its own (`%LOCALAPPDATA%Gatehouseebuilds`) and shows it in Explorer (`apps/host/src/rebuild.ts`, `POST /api/timeline/rebuild`): the newest checkpoint from before the step, written out from git's objects through a throw-away index, then every edit the session made after it up to the step, replayed from the audit log's record of each (Write, Edit, MultiEdit). No model is asked and nothing of the session runs: git is used only for read-tree, checkout-index, add, write-tree and rev-parse, and any other git command is refused. The result gives the rebuilt tree's git hash, so it can be compared with a checkpoint's, and says plainly what it could not replay, when it is then not exact: an edit recorded with part of it redacted (the rebuild stops before it), an edit whose text is not in the file, a notebook edit, and a shell command that ran after the checkpoint (named, never run; the next checkpoint holds what it changed). Recorded as `step_rebuilt`. Tests: rebuild.test.ts (a real repository: the tree rebuilt at a step has the later checkpoint's hash, the shell command is named and not run, and only those git commands run; a redacted edit stops the rebuild with the files as at the checkpoint).
- **Frontier D-057, an offline vulnerability snapshot, 12 Nov (the safe variant):** Codehouse ships no snapshot and fetches nothing. A file of OSV advisories (`osv-snapshot.json`, OSV's own format) placed in the managed folder (`C:ProgramDataCodehousemanaged`, which only an administrator can change) with its signature (`osv-snapshot.sig`, ECDSA P-256 over the file's bytes, base64, IEEE P1363 or DER) and the signer's public key (`osv-key.json`) is read when its files change (`apps/host/src/vuln-snapshot.ts`). A commit that adds or upgrades to a dependency version it lists (from the lockfiles as they are in HEAD and as they are now, D-056's reading; OSV's explicit versions and SEMVER or ECOSYSTEM ranges) goes to a person as `vulnerable-dependency`, naming each package, version and advisory id, and Extreme approval never approves it; in Auto and Accept edits the floor hook asks so it reaches canUseTool. An unsigned snapshot, one whose signature does not verify or one that is not OSV advisories is not used at all, nothing is matched against it, and Doctor says why; a lockfile that cannot be read while a snapshot is in use counts as a match (fail closed). It can only tighten. **Owner decision applied:** the spec has the snapshot signed with an IT key; the owner never asks IT (9 Nov), so whoever looks after this computer signs it, and the managed folder's permissions are what make its key trusted. A connector to a self-hosted or online OSV server is G28's, off until switched on, and can likewise only tighten. Tests: vuln-snapshot.test.ts (a known vulnerable version named with its advisory and a fixed one left alone; an unsigned and a tampered snapshot refused, matching nothing, with Doctor failing its check; a commit adding a vulnerable version sent to a person in Extreme approval).
- **Frontier D-003, the change evidence pack, 11 Nov:** for a change (what a session did since a checkpoint, up to a commit) one pack of what shows it was done properly (`apps/host/src/evidence.ts`): the decisions and approvals involved (who decided, the rule, the risk), each command run and how it ended (from the post hooks, with the exit code when it failed; a command from before Codehouse last started says not seen), the done-claims checked (D-009) and the sealed checks run (D-002), the dependencies changed with D-056's SBOM kept beside it, what D-062's analysis found, the files, and the checkpoint it starts from. Every item taken from the audit log carries that record's seq and hash. Coverage and mutation deltas are not recorded by any run yet, and the pack says so. A pack is made with each commit a session makes, and its hash goes into the commit's D-030 note (`evidence`); **Evidence...** in a session's menu shows the newest, makes one now (**Gather now**) and exports it (**Export, signed**): inside the session's signed timeline export (C-011), as evidence.json with evidence.sig over its bytes and head.json's, which both verifiers check, with every record it names (`verify-timeline.ts`, `verify-audit.ps1`). Packs and SBOMs are kept in `%LOCALAPPDATA%Gatehouseevidence`, each file named by its sha256 and redacted before it is written, read back only when its contents still match its name, for 90 days by default (Settings, Sessions, Evidence; `evidence.retentionDays`, read tolerantly); removing old ones touches only regular files directly in the folder, never through a link or a junction. Recorded as `evidence_pack_built`. Tests: evidence.test.ts (the pack's items and how each command ended, each tied to its record; the store's redaction, a changed file refused and the retention past a junction; the signed export passes both verifiers and one changed byte fails both; a record the evidence misnames fails both, the evidence_chain break check; 50 files in under 5 s).
- **Frontier D-029, audit anchoring, first part, 11 Nov:** once a day (at start when the last is a day old, then checked hourly) and with **Anchor now** on the Audit page, the audit chain's head (the last record's seq and hash) is signed and kept apart from the log, in `%LOCALAPPDATA%Gatehouseanchors`: a file per anchor and an annotated tag per anchor in a git repository of its own (`apps/host/src/anchors.ts`). Rewriting a record from before an anchor changes every hash after it, so the anchored record no longer has the anchored hash, even when the whole chain was recomputed to look intact. Both verifiers check each anchor's signature and its record (`packages/shared/src/audit/anchors.ts`; `scripts/verify-audit.ps1`, with `-Anchors`, by default the folder beside the audit folder; `npm run audit:verify -- --anchors=<folder>` likewise), and the Audit page says how many anchors match and when the last was made. Each is recorded as `anchor_signed`. **Deviation:** the local key is ECDSA P-256 (C-011's signer with a key file of its own, sealed with DPAPI), not Ed25519, because the PowerShell verifier runs in Windows PowerShell 5.1, whose .NET cannot check Ed25519; P-256 is as strong for this. These anchors are marked weaker, since anyone who can act as this Windows account could sign with the same key. Anchors signed with the phone's passkey are D-029b, below. Codehouse's own repository runs git with its own name and with commit and tag signing off for that repository only, so a signing program of yours never waits for a PIN. Tests: anchors.test.ts (an anchor recorded and kept with its tag; the same head not anchored twice; a record rewritten and the chain recomputed passes without the anchor and fails both verifiers with it; a changed anchor fails both).
- **Frontier D-062, local analysis of agent changes, 11 Nov:** the lines a change adds are parsed with C-031's Tree-sitter grammars (TypeScript, TSX, JavaScript, Python, C#, PowerShell) and checked for six kinds of mistake (`apps/host/src/sast.ts`): SQL built from strings (Severe when it goes straight into a query call, such as db.query with a template, cursor.execute with an f-string, FromSqlRaw with an interpolated string), a shell command built from input (exec, os.system, subprocess with shell=True), code run from a string (eval, Python's exec, Function, Invoke-Expression; Severe unless the argument is a literal), TLS checks switched off (rejectUnauthorized: false, verify=False, NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED, a certificate callback that answers true, check_hostname = False, CERT_NONE), weak cryptography (MD5, SHA-1, DES, RC2, RC4, createCipher) and a file path taken from a request (path.join, readFile, sendFile, open, Path.Combine with req.params, request.args or Request.Query). The tree is walked rather than queried per language, so one set of checks covers all six grammars. Changes lists each finding with its severity, file and line (`change_findings`, read only). A Severe one makes the session's next commit go to a person: canUseTool escalates it as `sast-severe`, which Extreme approval never approves, and in Auto and Accept edits the floor hook asks so it reaches canUseTool; a commit that cannot be read is treated as Severe (fail closed). The commit's added lines come from the same reading as the secret scan (`commitAdditions`, split out of `scanCommit`). Nothing runs: the code is only parsed. D-003's evidence pack will carry the findings. Tests: sast.test.ts (the seeded patterns in TypeScript, Python and C# with file and line; ordinary code and unchanged lines left alone; a commit's added lines read, and a commit that cannot be read rejected; a Severe commit sent to a person in Extreme approval, and an unreadable one too).
- **Frontier D-056, an SBOM per change, 11 Nov:** the Changes pane offers **Download the SBOM of these changes** when the change touches a lockfile: a CycloneDX 1.6 JSON document of each component the change adds, changes or removes (`codehouse:change`, with `codehouse:previousVersion` for a changed one, and the lockfile it came from), its package URL and, where the lockfile has a whole digest, its hash. Codehouse makes it itself (`apps/host/src/sbom.ts`) from the lockfiles as they were at the checkpoint and as they are now, read as text: npm (package-lock.json and npm-shrinkwrap.json, versions 1 to 3), yarn (classic and Berry), pnpm, Cargo, Poetry and uv, Go's go.sum, Composer, NuGet's packages.lock.json, Pipfile.lock and Gemfile.lock. Nothing runs and nothing is fetched; a lockfile that does not parse lists nothing. The session command is `change_sbom`, read only. CycloneDX 1.6 has no diff document, so the change is a property of each component. D-003's evidence pack will carry the same document. **Dependencies:** `ajv` 8.20.0 and `ajv-formats` 3.0.1 as exact devDependencies of the Host, for the test only, to check the document against the official CycloneDX 1.6 schema (copied into `apps/host/test/fixtures/cyclonedx`, Apache 2.0); both were already installed through fastify and the MCP SDK, so nothing new is downloaded, and nothing in Codehouse loads them. Tests: sbom.test.ts (added, changed and removed from npm's lockfile with the workspace's own packages left out; the other lockfiles; the document validates against the schema, and one missing its format does not).
- **Frontier D-026, policy regression from real history, 11 Nov:** every save of the rules (the rules file in Advanced, the Library and Your choices) replays the last 90 days of real decisions from the audit log (`regressionsOf` and `regressionGate` in `apps/host/src/api.ts`). A record that was refused or asked about and that the new rules would allow, while the rules in force would not, blocks the save: nothing is written, the desktop lists those calls (what they were, before and after, and the rule that would allow them) and `policy_regression_blocked` is recorded. Typing the phrase shown (`allow` and the count, so the number you saw is the number you accept) on the desktop saves it anyway, recorded first as `policy_regression_accepted`; if that cannot be recorded nothing is saved. The admin API is the desktop's alone, so the phone and push channels cannot confirm one. A change that only tightens saves at once. The Library's body is your rules alone, so its phrase comes as `?confirm=`. One panel on the desktop for all three (`components/RegressionConfirm.tsx`). Comparing with the rules in force, not only the recorded outcome, means a call already allowed by a rule you remembered since is not blamed on the change. Not a golden case: it decides a save, not a call. Tests: api.test.ts (a flipping rule blocks with the list and is recorded, the wrong phrase saves nothing, the right one saves and records both events, only 90 days count, a tightening saves at once; the Library with ?confirm=) and policy-regression.test.tsx (the panel lists the calls and saves only with the phrase).
- **Frontier D-061, the untrusted content scanner, 11 Nov:** results from the web, from servers that are not Codehouse's own, and from untrusted files (D-015's list) are read for what prompt injection looks like (`apps/host/src/injection-scan.ts`): instructions aimed at the agent (ignore the previous instructions, you are now, do not tell the user, the assistant must now run), tool-call syntax (function-call and tool-use tags, chat-template markers, tool_use keys), hidden text (runs of zero-width characters, Unicode tag characters, text styled invisible) and encoded payloads (base64 of 240 characters or more, base64 data addresses). A finding raises the session's taint, so its next network call (a network-capable command or WebFetch) asks whatever it carries (D-016's tainted-egress, never approved by Extreme approval); the result Claude reads has the findings taken out and marked, with a note to treat the rest as untrusted information (the post hook's `updatedToolOutput`; see docs/sdk-notes.md, where it is now recorded); `injection_flagged` is recorded with the kinds, never the result; and the session's pane shows what was found in each result (the quarantine view). Tests: injection-scan.test.ts (a seeded page flagged for instructions, tool syntax and hidden text, and cleaned with the rest kept; hidden characters and a long payload; 20 ordinary pages, fewer than 2 flagged (not yet run: the laptop was short of memory); the hook hands Claude the cleaned result, decides nothing, and the next curl asks while npm test does not).
- **Frontier D-017, subagent boundary labels, 11 Nov:** a subagent's own reads already pass through the session's hooks, so what it reads is taint like any other; now its report does too. The floor hook notes when a Task or Agent call starts (`subagentStarted`), and when it finishes (`subagentFinished`, from the post hook), if anything untrusted was read meanwhile its report counts as read from outside: a command it gives word for word is B32's copied command, and an address, email or token in it carries "the report of a subagent that read untrusted content" as its source for D-016. A subagent that read nothing untrusted taints nothing. Tests: subagent-taint.test.ts (a subagent reads an injected CONTRIBUTING file, and the main agent's remote and push with the address it reported escalates; a command it reports is caught as copied; a subagent that read nothing untrusted changes nothing).
- **Frontier D-016, the taint-tracked egress guard, 11 Nov:** B32's provenance now follows values, not only whole commands (`apps/host/src/provenance.ts`): the web addresses (and their hosts), email addresses and long tokens (32 characters or more of letters, digits and +/=_-) in what a session reads from outside (a fetched page, named by its address; another server's tool) and in untrusted files (D-015's list, named by the file), each kept with where it came from (3,000 at most, oldest out). A network-capable call that carries one (curl, wget, Invoke-WebRequest and Invoke-RestMethod, BITS, scp, sftp, ftp, rsync, ssh, nc, telnet, publishing to npm, PyPI or NuGet, a GitHub release upload, git push, adding or changing a remote; WebFetch to such an address), or a write outside the session's folder whose input carries one, escalates as `tainted-egress` at risk 70 or more, the reason naming the value and its source; Extreme approval never approves it (NEVER_AUTO_APPROVED), and like B32 it can only escalate, never allow, and leaves a deny a deny. Addresses on this computer (localhost, 127.x, ::1) are never somewhere else. Golden: apps/host/test/golden/taint-egress.yaml (11 cases of propagation: a README's address in curl, a host on another path, a page's address fetched, an email in a send, a remote added, a token written outside the project and inside it, a read, an address nothing mentioned, localhost, a push to the existing remote). Tests: taint-egress.test.ts (the golden cases, a deny left alone, Extreme never approving, the values read).
- **Frontier D-085, the data-boundary hand-off check, 11 Nov:** each project's content has a classification, public, internal, confidential or restricted (`dataClassification` in Gatehouse settings, Settings, Sessions, Data classification: a default, internal unless set, and folder names with their own). Carrying a chat on with another model (fallback_used, the one hand-off of content between models today) is refused, and recorded as `handoff_denied` with the models and the classification, when the target's card does not allow it (`refuseHandoff` in boundary.ts): a model whose card (C-016) is in date may be handed what the card's new "most sensitive content it may be handed" allows, internal unless it says; a model with no card, or a stale one, may be handed internal content at most. Decision for the owner to confirm: internal content, the default, still goes where it went before with no card, so nothing that works today stops; confidential and restricted content needs a card in date. Comparisons send your own new message to each model, not one model's content to another, and are left as they were. Tests: boundary.test.ts (a project's classification; confidential to a model with a stale card or none denied with the reason and recorded; internal anywhere, confidential where a card in date allows it, restricted not).
- **Frontier D-075, the loop and thrash detector, 11 Nov:** G19's loop guard tells first now. The third time the same call fails the same way in a row with nothing changed in between, or an edit is made and undone a second time, Claude is told what it repeated, in what it reads with that call's result (the post hook's `additionalContext`, which decides nothing), and `thrash_warned` is recorded. If it does the same again (the next same failure, or the edit undone once more), the turn stops as G19's did, `thrash_paused` is recorded (it replaces loop_stopped), and the phone and push channels are told. No progress for a number of turns is already Extreme approval's stop after two turns in a row that do no work (owner, 31 Oct). The test SDK now hands back what a post hook adds, as Claude Code does. Tests: loop-guard.test.ts (warn at the third, stop at the fourth, counts smoothed; a fix cycle never counts; an edit undone twice warns and a third time stops; on a real session, the message with the third failure's result, the stop after the next, both recorded and told).
- **Frontier D-011, flake quarantine, 11 Nov:** when the verifier's seeded rerun of the tests fails, it runs them up to four more times, each with a fresh seed (`#rerunForFlakes` in sessions.ts; it stops early if a run cannot be made, is stopped or times out). Passes and failures mixed label the claim flaky: it stays contradicted (the tests cannot be relied on), says how many of the runs passed, and carries every run's seed (`runs`, `flaky`), in the session's claims, the event and claim_verified; the session's card says Claim contradicted: flaky tests. Decision: flaky is a mark on a contradicted claim, not a new outcome, so an older screen or phone that reads the claims is not broken by a value it does not know. Skipping or deleting a flaky test is a change that weakens the tests, so D-001 escalates it as it does any skip (golden 15-test-integrity). Tests: seeds.test.ts (a run that fails and passes in turn is flaky after five runs, with five different seeds and the counts; one that always fails is not flaky).
- **Frontier D-010, randomised check seeds, 11 Nov:** when the done-claim verifier (D-009) runs a test command again for a claim that the tests pass or that something is fixed, it gives the run a fresh seed (`apps/host/src/seeds.ts`): `CODEHOUSE_SEED` in its environment (a Codehouse action now takes extra variables, with credentials taken out of them as out of the rest), and the runner's own flag where Codehouse knows the runner: Vitest's `--sequence.seed` with `--sequence.shuffle`, and Jest's `--seed`, named in the command or behind npm test (read from the project's test script). A command that already names a seed is left alone, and a runner Codehouse does not know gets the variable only. The seed is the same for every claim of one reply, is recorded in claim_verified, and a contradiction names it, so the failure can be run again with it. The rules allow the seeded `npm test -- ...` as they allow npm test. Tests: seeds.test.ts (the flags for each runner and none for others; on a real session, a test written against one seed passes as Claude ran it, fails the rerun, and the seed is in the event and the record).
- **Frontier C-016, a verified data-boundary card per model, 10 Nov:** B17's cards (where your words go: what is kept and for how long, training, region, zero data retention, when and where it was checked) can now be recorded for one model by its id as well as for a provider (`providers.boundaries.models` in Gatehouse settings, saved by adding and recorded first), and a model's own card says more than its provider's. A message to a session or chat (the server's prompt command) is refused when the card it goes by is stale: checked longer ago than `providers.boundaries.maxAgeDays` allows (180 by default, set in Settings beside the cards), or recorded with no date (`apps/host/src/boundary.ts`, `refuseStaleSend`); the refusal names the model, the date and the limit, and is recorded as `boundary_stale_refused` with the model, never the message. A stale card says so in Settings. Decision for the owner to confirm: a provider or model with no card at all is not refused, since older versions kept none and refusing them would stop every session the day this arrives; the stricter rule (no card, no send) would need a setting of its own. Tests: boundary.test.ts (a model's card before its provider's; stale by age, by a shorter limit and by having no date, and refused; no card not refused; on a real session, a stale card refuses the send and records it without the message, a fresh one lets it through).
- **Frontier C-012, the policy dry run, 10 Nov:** when Claude asks to leave Plan mode, the plan is read for the calls it names (`apps/host/src/plan-dry-run.ts`: each line of a shell code block, or of an unlabelled one that reads as a command; a command in backticks in the prose; a backticked file after a verb such as update, edit, create or add, as an Edit or a Write; at most 50) and each is judged by the live policy in the Host's own engine, which runs nothing and records nothing as a decision. The plan's card (desktop and phone) then says "An estimate, from the N steps the plan names, against your rules now: a would be allowed, b would ask you, c would be refused. What Claude runs may differ, and each call is still judged when it is made.", and the plan's record (plan_decision, by you or by Extreme approval) holds the three counts. A plan that names no call, or an engine that fails, shows no estimate. Tests: plan-dry-run.test.ts (the calls read from a plan; the counts and their words, with nothing run: a file the plan would write is not written, a folder it would delete is still there, no record is written; the card's reason and the record's counts on a real Plan-mode session).
- **Frontier C-011, the signed timeline export, 10 Nov:** Export, signed, in a session's Timeline, saves `codehouse-timeline-<date>.zip` (`/api/timeline/export?session=`, `apps/host/src/timeline-export.ts`): G30's audit bundle (the month files as they are, head.json, verify-audit.ps1), and `timeline.json` (the session's C-010 timeline, made from those records, already redacted), `timeline.sig` (ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256, IEEE P1363, base64, over timeline.json's bytes followed by head.json's), `timeline-key.json` (the public key, a JWK) and `verified.json` (what both verifiers said of the export when it was made). The key pair is made on first use; its private half is sealed for this Windows user (DPAPI) in the data folder. Decision: ECDSA P-256 rather than Ed25519, because Windows PowerShell 5.1's .NET Framework checks ECDSA and has no Ed25519, and no PowerShell version is a hard dependency (owner, 4 Nov). The screen shows the key's fingerprint (`/api/timeline/key`), which VERIFY.txt names too. Both verifiers check it: `verifyTimelineExport` (packages/shared/src/audit/verify-timeline.ts, run by npm run audit:verify) and verify-audit.ps1, written separately, check the signature, that each entry's records are in the export, belong to the session and are in order, that the entries are in audit order, and that each says what its last record says. Each export is recorded (`timeline_exported`: entries, size, fingerprint, whether both checks passed), and is not given if it cannot be. Found while building it: verify-audit.ps1 refused any record with the channel push or the decider human-push or extreme-mode, which the Node schema allows, so a log with one Extreme approval failed the PowerShell check; it accepts them now, and a test keeps its lists the same as the schema's. Tests: timeline-export.test.ts (the export's files, both verifiers accepting it, the same key next time; one changed byte of the timeline, the head or a record failing both), audit-bundle.test.ts (the two verifiers' lists).
- **Frontier C-010, the audit-backed session timeline, 10 Nov:** Timeline... in a coding session's menu, beside Walkthrough, shows everything the session did from the audit log (`/api/timeline?session=`, `apps/host/src/timeline.ts`): each call once, in audit order (by the chain's sequence number, across month files, never by clock), joined by its tool use id and ending in its last record's decision, rule, risk, who decided and the channel; what was not a call (a mode change, a pause, Extreme approval carrying on) as an event of its own. Each entry links to its records (Open record, on the Audit page), an edit's file (its diff against the checkpoint before the call, from checkpoint_preview), the checkpoint taken while it waited (the latest one in the session's folder within the call's records, or else the next call after it; checkpoints are git refs, not audit records), and Try in policy, which opens the Approver page's Try a request with the call filled in: the replay the item names, since the policy replay (C-012 and D-026) takes a rules file, not one call. What it shows is what was recorded, already redacted. Tests: timeline.test.ts (1,000 chained records over two months, 800 of them the session's: 400 entries in strictly rising order, each with its last decision and approver, none of the other session's; events apart, an edit's file, a checkpoint given to the call that waited for it).
- **Frontier D-064, install-script default deny, 10 Nov:** an npm, pnpm, yarn or bun install (install, i, add, ci, and yarn on its own) without --ignore-scripts is read for the scripts it would run, offline (`packages/policy/src/install-scripts.ts`): for each package it names, the preinstall, install and postinstall in node_modules/<name>/package.json, or the lockfile's hasInstallScript when the package is not here to read; for a bare install, the project's own preinstall, install, postinstall and prepare, and every package the lockfile says has one. Any script escalates as `install-scripts` (risk 78) with each script's text in the reason, and Extreme approval never approves it (NEVER_AUTO_APPROVED). The verdict carries `withoutScripts`, the same command with --ignore-scripts after each install in it and nothing else changed (`withoutScripts`; a new optional Verdict field), which the card offers as Run without scripts: it is sent as the person's edited input, so canUseTool checks it again through the floor and the Approver and a deny blocks it (invariant 10); nothing is rewritten silently. Decisions for the owner to confirm: Extreme approval never approves an install that runs scripts, though a session building this repository then waits for you on npm ci (esbuild has one); a package not installed yet whose lockfile entry says nothing is not counted (D-053 already asks about a new one); pip, cargo and dotnet build steps are not read. Golden 23-install-scripts (6). Tests: packages/policy/test/install-scripts.test.ts (what each kind of install would run, with the text; --ignore-scripts added to each install only), apps/host/test/install-scripts.test.ts (Run without scripts in Extreme approval goes to a person, is evaluated again as the edited input and runs with --ignore-scripts; a deny on the re-check blocks it). Break checks: an install script not seen fails the policy's install-scripts.test.ts, and Run without scripts not checked again fails the host's.
- **Frontier D-064, install-script default deny, 10 Nov:** an npm, pnpm, yarn or bun install (install, i, add, ci, and yarn on its own) without --ignore-scripts is read for the scripts it would run, offline (`packages/policy/src/install-scripts.ts`): for each package it names, the preinstall, install and postinstall in node_modules/<name>/package.json, or the lockfile's hasInstallScript when the package is not here to read; for a bare install, the project's own preinstall, install, postinstall and prepare, and every package the lockfile says has one. Any script escalates as `install-scripts` (risk 78) with each script's text in the reason, and Extreme approval never approves it (NEVER_AUTO_APPROVED). The verdict carries `withoutScripts`, the same command with --ignore-scripts after each install in it and nothing else changed (`withoutScripts`; a new optional Verdict field), which the card offers as Run without scripts: it is sent as the person's edited input, so canUseTool checks it again through the floor and the Approver and a deny blocks it (invariant 10); nothing is rewritten silently. Decisions for the owner to confirm: Extreme approval never approves an install that runs scripts, though a session building this repository then waits for you on npm ci (esbuild has one); a package not installed yet whose lockfile entry says nothing is not counted (D-053 already asks about a new one); pip, cargo and dotnet build steps are not read. Golden 23-install-scripts (6). Tests: packages/policy/test/install-scripts.test.ts (what each kind of install would run, with the text; --ignore-scripts added to each install only), apps/host/test/install-scripts.test.ts (Run without scripts in Extreme approval goes to a person, is evaluated again as the edited input and runs with --ignore-scripts; a deny on the re-check blocks it). Break checks: an install script not seen fails the policy's install-scripts.test.ts, and Run without scripts not checked again fails the host's. Wave 1 gate, 10 Nov (the stack from D-025 to D-064 together): typecheck and lint passed; 213 of the related test files passed, and the four whose workers died part way with memory short (heldout, secret-commit-leak, api, engine) passed on their own, one at a time (89 tests); the rest skip on this machine.
- **Owner, 10 Nov, a turn after an Interrupt showed nothing:** "I interupted this ADAPT session with a message and it is still going but the details on the current activity being performed and the spinning circle in the left menu arent showing." The Host marked a session working only when it sent a message; after an Interrupt, Claude takes the message it already had and starts a turn by itself, so the session stayed idle on the screen until another message was sent. Any turn's first message (assistant or stream event) now marks an idle session working, until its result; this also covers a turn Claude starts when background work reports back. Test: self-started-turn.test.ts. Break check: the change taken out fails it.
- **Frontier D-059, instruction integrity pinning, 10 Nov:** what a project tells Claude (CLAUDE.md, CLAUDE.local.md and AGENTS.md at its root and in any folder, .claude/CLAUDE.md, .claude/settings.json and settings.local.json, and everything under .claude/skills, commands and agents) is pinned by SHA-256 in a pin file per project under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Gatehouse\instruction-pins\`, never in the repository, sealed with an HMAC whose key DPAPI keeps for this Windows user (as D-007's locks), with a copy of each version up to 256 KB kept by its hash for the diff (`apps/host/src/instruction-pins.ts`). A project seen for the first time is pinned as it is. An agent's change (a file tool's target, or a shell command's redirect into one or a command naming one other than to read it, D-007's reading, now shared as `changedByShell`) escalates as `instructions-edit` at risk 80 or more, recorded as `instructions_edit_escalated`, asked in Auto and Accept edits too, and never approved by Extreme approval (NEVER_AUTO_APPROVED); once a person allows it, the PostToolUse hook pins it again (`instructions_repinned`). A change made outside Codehouse (a pull, a checkout, another editor) is found at a session's start, before each of your messages (the server asks `checkInstructions` first) and before each turn Extreme approval would start, so a pull, merge, checkout or rebase inside a turn blocks the next one; it is recorded once as `instructions_changed` and the session and the screen are told. The desktop shows the diff of each file (InstructionsReview: in the new session form and above the prompt box) and "I have read it: approve the change" records `instructions_approved` before the pins move (`approveInstructions`). A pin file edited by hand or unreadable shows every file for review. Decisions for the owner to confirm: Extreme approval never approves an agent's change to the instructions, though building Codehouse inside Codehouse then waits for you on a CLAUDE.md edit (rare); a chat is not checked, since it reads no project's instructions; only the project's root and .claude folders are walked for outside changes (CLAUDE.md in a subfolder is guarded against agents but not watched for a pull). Tests: instruction-pins.test.ts (the files; first pin, an outside change shown as a diff, approve, a removal; an edited or broken pin file; what a call would change; on real sessions, an Extreme session's skill edit waits for a person and is pinned after, and a pulled CLAUDE.md blocks the open session's next turn and a new start until approved, recorded both ways). Break checks: an outside change not seen, and an agent's skill edit not asked, each fail instruction-pins.test.ts.
- **Frontier D-058, the secret commit block, 10 Nov:** G22's floor check, against D-058's acceptance. Added: a push is read too (`makesPush`; every commit on a local branch no remote has yet, `git log -p --branches --not --remotes`, so a key committed earlier by you or before Codehouse is not sent on by a session; a push it cannot read is asked, never allowed), and each finding names its line (from the diff's hunk headers, or the new file's line), so the refusal and the record hold the rule id (`secret-in-commit`), the file and the line only. Decision: a push reads every local branch no remote has, not only the one named, the stricter reading; a push you need despite a finding is yours, in the Terminal. Golden lines in `apps/host/test/golden/commit-secrets.yaml` (15: one per kind, and ordinary lines that must pass), the keys written as {NAME} and put together by the test, so the repository holds none. Tests: commit-scan.test.ts (the golden lines; a push and what it sends, nothing once a remote has it; a staged test AWS key denied in Manual, Plan, Accept edits and Auto, naming file and line, the value nowhere), secret-commit-leak.test.ts (secret_no_leak: on a real session in Extreme approval the commit and the push are refused by the floor, and the audit files, the records, the Host's log, the transcript events, the session's state and the tool results hold no six characters of the key). Break checks: a push not read, and the line not named, each fail commit-scan.test.ts. Tested with Wave 1, 10 Nov: an Anthropic key was also reported as an OpenAI one; the OpenAI pattern now leaves sk-ant- alone.
- **Frontier D-055, licence policy (safe variant), 10 Nov:** each dependency a call adds that the project does not use yet (D-053's reading of install commands and manifest edits) has its SPDX licence read from this computer only (`packages/policy/src/licences.ts`): the project's node_modules and package-lock.json, a .venv, venv or env in it (License-Expression, a short License line, or the common trove classifiers), the NuGet package folder (an expression licence in the nuspec) and Cargo's registry sources.
