The expression is evaluated (OR needs one side allowed, AND both, `X WITH exception` and `X+` count as X; `UNLICENSED` and `SEE LICENSE IN` are not licences) against the list: the managed policy's `licences` when it sets one (B37's signed overlay gains the field; an older version refuses a managed policy that has it, so sessions do not start, the stricter way), else `allowedLicences` in Gatehouse settings (Settings, Sessions, Licences; absent is the permissive default MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, ISC, 0BSD, Unlicense), and an `npm:name` line accepts one package whatever its licence. Outside the list escalates as `licence-not-allowed` (risk 82); unreadable as `licence-unknown` (74); D-053's reason and the licence's are joined, so the card says both whichever wins. canUseTool records `licence_escalated` before anything else and goes no further when it cannot. Decisions for the owner to confirm: Extreme approval never approves a licence outside the list (NEVER_AUTO_APPROVED) but may approve an unreadable one, recorded, since almost every new package is unreadable before it is installed; npm's download cache is not read (compressed archives under hashes); an owner-approved name (D-053) is still checked for its licence. Golden 22-licences (8 cases); D-053's golden cases for a name not near a popular one (dep6, dep12) now come back as licence-unknown, with D-053's reason. Tests: packages/policy/test/licences.test.ts (expressions; reading from each place; the findings), apps/host/test/licences.test.ts (recorded first; Extreme leaves a licence outside the list to you; an unrecorded one goes no further). Break checks: AND needing only one side fails licences.test.ts, and Extreme approval approving a licence outside the list fails the host's licences.test.ts. Tested with Wave 1, 10 Nov: an unreadable licence is said on D-053's card for a new package (risk 70, under dependency-new's 72) rather than leading every new package's card, and asks as licence-unknown on its own for a name the owner approved.
- **Frontier D-060, the agent-config tamper floor, 10 Nov:** a check built into the floor (`config-tamper.ts`, called by `checkCallFloor` from the engine and from `evaluateFloor`, which the PreToolUse hook runs in every mode, Extreme approval included) denies, as agent-config-tamper, a file tool's change to the keys that decide what a tool may do in .claude/settings*.json (permissions, hooks, defaultMode, allow, deny, ask, additionalDirectories, MCP server switches, apiKeyHelper) and .vscode/settings.json (autoApprove, chat.tools, chat.agent, Copilot's agent and MCP keys); a Write is compared with the file, and one that is not JSON is denied. Anything in .cursor, .codex, Codehouse's data folder (AppData/Local/Gatehouse, matched exactly, not a project folder named gatehouse) and the managed folder (ProgramData/Codehouse) is denied outright, from a file tool or the shell (in-place editors, redirects, Set-Content and the like, and a copy or move onto one). Golden: 21-config-tamper (10 cases). Threat model T81. Tests: config-tamper.test.ts (what is denied, and other settings in the same files left alone; the data folder matched exactly). Break checks: VS Code's auto approve let through fails config-tamper.test.ts, and a redirect into Cursor's settings let through fails the golden cases. Tested with Wave 1, 10 Nov: a shell redirect into a guarded file (`echo {} > .cursor/mcp.json`) was not caught, since segments do not keep redirect targets; the call's own text is now read for them.
- **Frontier D-096, WinGet and installer admission, 10 Nov:** winget install, upgrade and add, choco install and upgrade (and cinst), scoop install and update, msiexec, and Install-Module, Install-Package and Install-Script escalate as installer-admission at 80 (`installers.ts`) in every mode, with the package id, the source (a flag, a scoop bucket, an address or a local file) and a WinGet id's publisher part, said to be unchecked since nothing is looked up; the rule is in NEVER_AUTO_APPROVED, so Extreme approval waits for a person, and its card keeps its own reason. Listing (winget list) asks as before. Golden: 20-installers (8 cases); extreme.test.ts covers the never-approved rule with the others. Break checks: winget not seen fails the golden cases.
- **Frontier D-054, the lockfile diff guard, 10 Nov:** an agent's edit of any common lockfile, by Write, Edit or MultiEdit (a Write against the file as it is) or from the shell (an in-place editor or a redirect naming one), escalates as lockfile-edited at 70, raised to 80 when it gives a package an install script (`hasInstallScript`, shown with the script text read from node_modules where the package is there) or a source other than the usual registries (git, file, link, another host); the reason says no manifest change explains it (`lockfiles.ts`). Since an agent edits a lockfile by hand only to change what is installed without the manifest, every such edit is one the manifest does not explain; installs through the package manager are judged as installs (package-install, D-053, and D-064 for their scripts). Golden: 19-lockfiles (7 cases). Tests: lockfiles.test.ts (the postinstall script shown; a source off the registry named; a plain change still asks). Break checks: a new install script not raised to High fails lockfiles.test.ts. Tested with Wave 1, 10 Nov: yarn 1's `resolved "..."` lines were not read, so a source off the registry went unnamed; they are now.
- **Frontier D-053, dependency admission control (safe variant), 10 Nov:** with no network, the engine reads what a call adds to the project's dependencies (install commands of npm, pnpm, yarn, bun, pip, uv, poetry, pipenv, dotnet, nuget, cargo and go; edits of package.json, requirements files, pyproject.toml, .csproj, Cargo.toml and go.mod, a Write against the file as it is) and lets through only names the project already uses (its lockfiles and manifests, at the root and in the folder) or the owner approved (`dependencies.local.json` beside rules.local.yaml, in the policy hash). Others escalate as dependency-new (80 when a letter or two from a bundled popular name per ecosystem, which the reason names, by Damerau-Levenshtein distance; 72 otherwise), and a name on the bundled known-hallucination list is denied as dependency-hallucinated while the project does not use it; remember rules lift neither. The known-hallucination list ships with one documented case (huggingface-cli on PyPI, Lasso Security, 2024) with its source and date: the 2026 study (R15) counts 127 shared names but its text here does not list them, so none are made up; the owner adds names to `dependency-lists.ts` as they are published. List for IT (not built, per conflict 1 it would be a connector): existence, age and publisher checks against a registry. Golden: 18-dependencies (13 cases). Threat model T80. Tests: dependencies.test.ts (names from commands and manifest edits; known and approved names pass; near misses named). Break checks: every dependency counted as known fails dependencies.test.ts. Tested with Wave 1, 10 Nov: an install you remembered is not asked about again (only what the rest of the call adds is), and a known invented name is still refused; with the packages pack on, a package the project does not use yet still asks (decision for the owner to confirm: the packs still allow installs from a manifest or a lockfile and packages already used, and their descriptions say so).
- **Frontier D-030, approval lineage on commits, 10 Nov:** after a session's commit succeeds (the PostToolUse hook, `makesCommit`), the Host writes `commit_lineage_noted` naming the commit (`HostAudit.recordWithHash`, which returns the record's place in the chain) and then adds a git note under `refs/notes/codehouse` with the session, that record's sequence number and hash (the chain's head then) and the time (`lineage.ts`); a record that cannot be written makes no note. The note resolves to the record, the record names the commit, and the chain verifies. It is Codehouse's own bookkeeping, like a checkpoint, so it is not judged as the session's command; it runs with no credential, names Codehouse as the author of the notes commit only, and never pushes (notes are not pushed unless named). The evidence pack's hash joins the note with D-003 in Wave 2; trailers on commits from Codehouse's own git panel are left for when that panel commits. Tests: lineage.test.ts (in a real repository: the note on HEAD resolves to commit_lineage_noted, which names the commit, and the chain verifies; on refs/notes/codehouse, with no push configured; no note when the record cannot be written or there is no commit). Break checks: a note made without its audit record fails lineage.test.ts.
- **Frontier D-025, the dead-man timer for Extreme approval, 10 Nov:** off by default; turned on for one Extreme session from its menu (`set_deadman`, 1 to 240 minutes, recorded as `deadman_set`). Every desktop command counts as hearing from you (`SessionManager.heartbeat`, from the server's command handler), and so does every verified, signed action from the phone, at the moment its nonce is spent (`PhoneService`); the phone also has I'm still here, a new signed envelope type, `heartbeat`, that names no request, session or routine and lives as briefly as a kill (`verify.ts`), recorded as `phone_heartbeat`. Without a sign of you within the time, an Extreme session records `extreme_heartbeat_missed`, pauses (`paused.reason` heartbeat, which the limit pauser never starts again and only you do, which is itself hearing from you), and the phone and ntfy are told. A session not in Extreme approval is never paused by it. Tests: dead-man.test.ts (with N = 1 an Extreme session pauses within the equivalent of 70 seconds, recorded, started again only by you; heartbeats, as the phone sends, keep it going; off by default and never a non-Extreme session), with the minute shortened for the test. Break checks: the dead-man timer never firing fails dead-man.test.ts.
- **Frontier D-021, the rubber-stamp detector, 10 Nov:** the desktop's approvals carry how long the card was in view and whether the full card was open (`shownMs`, `opened`; a row of the queue sends opened false). The Host keeps a `RubberStamp` per desktop connection (`rubber-stamp.ts`): three High or Severe approvals in a row each within 800 ms, or ten in a row from unopened cards, arm it. Armed, the next High approval is refused with the code friction and `approval_friction_shown` is recorded; the card then shows a Look again panel above its details (the command, Also runs and the effects already on it) with Yes, allow it and Allow, and for a day in this folder only (a C-013 grant), both enabled after two seconds; the Host takes the deliberate approval only two seconds or more after the friction was shown, and it disarms. A deny is never slowed and ends a run. Tests: rubber-stamp.test.ts (three fast High arm it, slow or Moderate ones do not; ten unopened arm it; a deny or a deliberate approval resets it), server.test.ts (on the desktop socket: three fast approvals, the fourth refused with friction while its deny goes through, the fifth refused until two seconds have passed and then taken, two frictions recorded).

Gate, 10 Nov: 1,910 of 1,918 related tests passed in a 52-minute run on a loaded machine; the 8 that failed were in four files, and on their own server.test.ts, marketplace.test.ts and scripted-run.test.ts passed. provenance.test.ts failed each time: on this laptop every process took about 2 s to start (git, cmd and whoami alike, with nine hung tzutil processes and orphaned shells about, now stopped), so the checkpoint before an escalation took 16 s against the test's 15 s wait; it now waits 60 s and passes. Nothing in D-021 was at fault.
- **Frontier D-018, the risk budget, 10 Nov:** opt-in and off by default (`riskBudget` in Gatehouse settings, Settings, Sessions, Risk budget; tolerant: off is kept beside the points). Each session starts with the points; an allow given without a person spends them: Extreme approval's approval of an escalation (`onAutoApproved`, after it is recorded) and a call Claude Code approved by itself in Auto or Accept edits (the PostToolUse hook, for a call that had a verdict and never reached canUseTool), 1 point at Moderate risk (40 to 69) and 3 at High (70 and over). Decision: a person's own approvals spend nothing, since the budget is a limit on what runs without you. At zero `risk_budget_exhausted` is recorded, the phone and ntfy are told, an Extreme session pauses (`paused.reason` "risk", which the limit pauser never starts again; Start now does, with the budget full) and a Manual-family session goes back to Manual. Your own message fills the budget again. Details shows what is left. Tests: risk-budget.test.ts (an Extreme session paused after 3, recorded, not started again by a low plan limit, started by you with a full budget; an Auto session back to Manual so the next Moderate call asks; a message fills it again; off by default).
- **Frontier D-015, the allow-rule provenance lock, 10 Nov:** the provenance labels (B32) also keep files that are not the project's own words, read in the session (`UNTRUSTED_FILES`: README and Markdown files, node_modules, vendor and third_party; `untrustedFiles` in Gatehouse settings changes it), apart from what was read from outside, so a command named in a README is not on that account escalated as copied (B32 unchanged). `Provenance.mentions` names a rule's command (eight characters or more), file (its name, or its path from the folder) or site found in either. canUseTool's Remember is refused when it does, and a whole site is remembered only when the person typed it to confirm (`rememberFor.confirmScope`); the call is allowed once, `remember_refused_provenance` is recorded, and the screen is told why. The card's Remember preview says so before the person chooses, and asks a site to be typed. Accepting a suggested rule is refused (409) while any open session read something that mentions it. Tests: remember-provenance.test.ts (what is named and what is not; remember_provenance: a README asking for a broad allow cannot make a rule, the card says why, and it is recorded; a site remembered only when typed).
- **Frontier D-013, effect-complete approval cards, 10 Nov:** before a shell call is decided the engine expands what it would also run (`packages/policy/src/expand.ts`, read only, never run): npm, pnpm and yarn scripts with their pre, post and install lifecycle scripts and the scripts they call (five levels, loops stopped), Makefile recipes and the targets they need, the git hooks a command fires (core.hooksPath or .git/hooks; skipped with --no-verify, which D-001 already asks about), .cmd and .bat wrappers, the tool npx would run (installed in node_modules/.bin, or a fetch, which cannot be read), local dotnet tools from the manifest, and the PowerShell profile for a PowerShell call without -NoProfile. Each line is judged like the call (`PolicyEngine.#withEffects`): a deny on any line denies it, with the line and where it came from in the reason; a line a rule escalates makes it ask if stricter; what cannot be read is `unknown-effects` (risk 60). Decision (recorded for the owner): a line no rule speaks of does not by itself make the call ask, since otherwise every npm test whose script runs an unlisted tool, and every PowerShell call whose profile has an ordinary line, would wait for a person; it is stricter than before and meets the acceptance cases. The lines ride on the verdict (`expanded`, `unknownEffects`, in `Verdict`), so the broker redacts them with the rest, and on the phone's sealed payload only when present (a request without them hashes as before); the desktop card and the phone card show them under Also runs. The golden runner now places several files for a case and points the profile folder at an empty fixture, so no case depends on the machine's own profile. Golden: 17-effects-expand, 30 cases (npm, npx, make, git hooks, wrappers, dotnet tools). Threat model T79 (approval laundering). Tests: expand.test.ts (effects_expand: npm test whose script runs curl asks, with the line on the verdict and the phone payload; a git commit whose pre-commit hook runs a denied command is denied; a call that runs nothing more is unchanged; the PowerShell profile read, and not with -NoProfile; scripts calling scripts, a loop stopped, nothing run).
- **Frontier section 6, Codehouse's own actions, and D-009, the done-claim verifier, 10 Nov:** a command Codehouse runs itself now goes through one runner (`codehouse-action.ts`, `runCodehouseAction`): not started while the kill switch is on; judged by the Approver as the same Bash command from the session (a deny refused, an escalation sent to a person through the broker, except where Extreme approval would approve it, never for a failure to decide; an edited command refused, since Codehouse runs only what it showed); run with every credential taken out of its environment (`withoutCredentials`: tokens, secrets, passwords, API and access keys, Anthropic and Claude Code variables); run in a job at the session's resource level through Codehouse-Limits.exe, or a plain child process where the helper cannot be had; stopped at once when the kill switch is engaged; recorded as `codehouse_action` with its outcome. Fix to D-002: its held-out runs now go through it (they had been run at a fixed Medium level, unjudged, with the full environment and without watching the kill switch). D-009 (`claims.ts`): a reply is read for claims that the work is done (tests pass, build succeeds, lint or typecheck clean, fixed, all green; a claim taken back in its own sentence, such as "not all tests pass" or "should pass once", is not one); the command each rests on is the last of the turn's commands that checks it (only the part that runs the check, in the folder it ran in, so "cd app && npm test | tail" reruns "npm test" in app and tail's exit does not decide), else the project's own script; Codehouse reruns it as above at the session's level and stamps it Verified, Contradicted or Unverified (no command, refused, stopped, timed out, or tests weakened in the session, D-001) in the transcript, the session's Details and its dashboard tile, and records `claim_verified`. Deviations: the recap is not stamped yet (it is Claude's own text; the transcript line sits beside it), and the evidence pack comes with Wave 2. Tests: codehouse-action.test.ts (allowed and run without credentials at the session's level and recorded; deny refused; escalation asked, and approved only by a person or by Extreme approval by its rules; kill switch before and during), claims.test.ts (claims found and taken back; the part of a line rerun; Contradicted after a failing run, Verified, Unverified with nothing to run).
- **Frontier D-018, the risk budget, 10 Nov:** opt-in and off by default (`riskBudget` in Gatehouse settings, Settings, Sessions, Risk budget; tolerant: off is kept beside the points). Each session starts with the points; an allow given without a person spends them: Extreme approval's approval of an escalation (`onAutoApproved`, after it is recorded) and a call Claude Code approved by itself in Auto or Accept edits (the PostToolUse hook, for a call that had a verdict and never reached canUseTool), 1 point at Moderate risk (40 to 69) and 3 at High (70 and over). Decision: a person's own approvals spend nothing, since the budget is a limit on what runs without you. At zero `risk_budget_exhausted` is recorded, the phone and ntfy are told, an Extreme session pauses (`paused.reason` "risk", which the limit pauser never starts again; Start now does, with the budget full) and a Manual-family session goes back to Manual. Your own message fills the budget again. Details shows what is left. Tests: risk-budget.test.ts (an Extreme session paused after 3, recorded, not started again by a low plan limit, started by you with a full budget; an Auto session back to Manual so the next Moderate call asks; a message fills it again; off by default). Break checks: Extreme approvals not spending, no drop to Manual, a budget when none is set, the session itself starting a risk pause at a limit reset, and the limit pauser asking it to, each fail their tests. The two last guard the same thing twice, so each has its own test.
- **Frontier D-015, the allow-rule provenance lock, 10 Nov:** the provenance labels (B32) also keep files that are not the project's own words, read in the session (`UNTRUSTED_FILES`: README and Markdown files, node_modules, vendor and third_party; `untrustedFiles` in Gatehouse settings changes it), apart from what was read from outside, so a command named in a README is not on that account escalated as copied (B32 unchanged). `Provenance.mentions` names a rule's command (eight characters or more), file (its name, or its path from the folder) or site found in either. canUseTool's Remember is refused when it does, and a whole site is remembered only when the person typed it to confirm (`rememberFor.confirmScope`); the call is allowed once, `remember_refused_provenance` is recorded, and the screen is told why. The card's Remember preview says so before the person chooses, and asks a site to be typed. Accepting a suggested rule is refused (409) while any open session read something that mentions it. Tests: remember-provenance.test.ts (what is named and what is not; remember_provenance: a README asking for a broad allow cannot make a rule, the card says why, and it is recorded; a site remembered only when typed). Break checks: remember_provenance (the mention not checked), an untrusted file not kept, and a site remembered without being typed each fail their tests.

Tests: expand.test.ts (effects_expand: npm test whose script runs curl asks, with the line on the verdict and the phone payload; a git commit whose pre-commit hook runs a denied command is denied; a call that runs nothing more is unchanged; the PowerShell profile read, and not with -NoProfile; scripts calling scripts, a loop stopped, nothing run). Break checks: effects_expand (no expansion at all), a denied line not denying, unknown effects let through, git hooks not read, and pre and post scripts left out each fail their tests. Found while running the golden suite: the golden workspace is now a real project (a JSON package.json with no scripts, its usual tools installed), since an unreadable package.json is itself an unknown effect and npx with a tool not installed would fetch it.
- **Frontier section 6, Codehouse's own actions, and D-009, the done-claim verifier, 10 Nov:** a command Codehouse runs itself now goes through one runner (`codehouse-action.ts`, `runCodehouseAction`): not started while the kill switch is on; judged by the Approver as the same Bash command from the session (a deny refused, an escalation sent to a person through the broker, except where Extreme approval would approve it, never for a failure to decide; an edited command refused, since Codehouse runs only what it showed); run with every credential taken out of its environment (`withoutCredentials`: tokens, secrets, passwords, API and access keys, Anthropic and Claude Code variables); run in a job at the session's resource level through Codehouse-Limits.exe, or a plain child process where the helper cannot be had; stopped at once when the kill switch is engaged; recorded as `codehouse_action` with its outcome. Fix to D-002: its held-out runs now go through it (they had been run at a fixed Medium level, unjudged, with the full environment and without watching the kill switch).
