Break checks: credentials left in the environment, the command not judged, the kill switch ignored mid-run, the session's level not used, a failing check called Verified, and a claim with no command run anyway each fail their tests. Separately, npm audit (9 moderate) are all in the ESLint tooling through minimatch, which ships in nothing: npm audit --omit=dev finds none. ESLint and typescript-eslint are to be upgraded in a slice of their own.
- **Frontier D-008, pinned counterexamples, 10 Nov:** the PostToolUse hook gives each shell command's output to the session (`onToolOutput`), which reads it for a shrunk counterexample (`counterexamples.ts`: fast-check's `Counterexample: [...]` with the failing file and test from vitest or jest, Hypothesis's `Falsifying example: test(...)` with its pytest file, FsCheck's `Shrunk:` line, Go's and libFuzzer's saved failing input) and offers each on the pane (`counterexamples` in the session's state; Pin as a regression test, or Dismiss). Pin (`pin_counterexample`, desktop only; `counterexample-pins.ts`): for fast-check and Hypothesis Codehouse copies the failing property test, with the file's imports, into a file of its own beside it (`<name>.regression-<case>.test.<ext>`, `test_<name>_regression_<case>.py`), named after the case and running only it (fast-check `examples` with `numRuns: 0`; Hypothesis `@example` with `@settings(phases=[Phase.explicit])`); records `counterexample_pinned` first and writes nothing if the record fails; then locks the file (D-007). For FsCheck it asks Claude to write the test in a named file and locks it, with the record, once Claude has written it; for a fuzz run it locks the input the run saved. Dependency: none; the fixture stands a few lines in for fast-check, as fast-check documents examples and numRuns, rather than adding fast-check to the repository. Tests: counterexamples.test.ts (reading the four kinds; in a fixture, a failing fast-check property gives a regression test that fails before the fix, every time, and passes after it, recorded and locked; the Hypothesis copy; Claude asked and the file locked once written, another file not taken for it; nothing written when the pin cannot be recorded). Break checks: the counterexample left out of the test, the test written before it is recorded, the test not locked, and Claude's file not locked each fail their tests.
- **Frontier D-007, locked acceptance tests, 10 Nov:** the owner locks a test from Changes (Lock beside a file) or from Details, Locked tests, on the desktop only (`lock_test`, `unlock_test`, `locks_accept`, `locks_state`). Each project's lock file is under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Gatehouse\locks\`, never in the repository, with the SHA-256 of each locked file and an HMAC made with a key sealed by DPAPI (`test-locks.ts`); a built-in floor rule (`test-locks`) denies the folder to sessions. canUseTool turns any verdict but a deny into the escalation locked-test (risk 90) when the call would change a locked test: a file tool aimed at it, or a shell command naming it other than to read or run it (cat, grep, git diff, a test runner), redirects included; it is recorded first (`locked_test_escalated`) and refused if the record fails; Extreme approval never approves it (NEVER_AUTO_APPROVED), and the floor hook asks about it in Auto and Accept edits. After a person approves such a change, the PostToolUse hook pins the file as it now is (`test_lock_repinned`; a deleted one is unlocked). A lock file that does not match its seal makes every test file count as locked (fail closed). A locked test changed outside Codehouse stops a new coding session in that project (`LockedTestsChangedError`, a card on the start screen) until the owner accepts the new versions; sessions coming back after a restart carry on. Audited: `test_locked`, `test_unlocked`, `test_locks_accepted`, `locked_test_escalated`, `test_lock_repinned`. Deviation: whole files only for now; locking one case within a file needs the code outline (C-031, before Wave 2) to know where a case begins and ends. Threat model T78. Tests: test-locks.test.ts (what a call touches and what only reads or runs; the tampered lock file; a change outside Codehouse found and accepted; extreme_locked_tests; asked in Auto; no session while a locked test changed outside). Break checks: no check in canUseTool, Extreme approving a locked test, Auto not asked, a tampered lock file trusted, no check at session start, and a shell rm not counted each fail their tests. Found while running them: paths are compared as real paths, since a folder named by its 8.3 short name (PETERJ~1.KEL) or a link otherwise looked like another folder.
- **Frontier D-002, sealed held-out checks, 10 Nov:** tests the owner adds for a project from the desktop only (Details, Held-out checks; `heldout_*` commands on the desktop's socket, never the phone's or a push channel's) are sealed with DPAPI, the index of their names too, under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Gatehouse\sealed\<project>\` (`heldout.ts`). A floor rule built into Codehouse and checked before the rules file (`BUILTIN_FLOOR`, `sealed-checks`, so neither an edited nor an older rules file leaves it out) denies every tool any path under `Gatehouse/sealed` or `Gatehouse/heldout-runs`, in every mode. A run, on demand or when a session says its queue is empty, unseals them into a fresh folder under `Gatehouse\heldout-runs\` (outside every worktree), runs the project's own command with `{dir}` in the project's folder inside a job at the Medium level (Codehouse-Limits.exe, `limitedCommand`) for at most ten minutes, reads the counts from the runner's summary (vitest, jest, pytest, mocha, dotnet test, Pester), and removes the folder without following a link or junction out of it (`removeTreeNoFollow`). Only the counts reach the session (an event on its screen, `heldout_result`, and Tell Claude the counts sends just that line); `sealed_check_run` records the counts, and `sealed_check_changed` records an add, a removal or a new command without the name or the content; the names and the output go back only to the desktop that asked. Golden: 16-sealed-checks (12 denials across Read, Glob, Grep, Edit, Write, Bash and PowerShell, both slashes, a variable and another case, and one look-alike allowed). Threat model T77. Tests: heldout.test.ts (counts of six runners; sealed on disk with no plain name; a bad name and a command without {dir} refused; a junction in the run folder unlinked, its target kept; sealed_no_leak on a real session; the floor in Manual, Plan, Accept edits, Auto and Extreme). Break checks: the built-in floor rule left out, the run folder left behind, the names told to the session and a check stored unsealed each fail their tests; following links needs both of removeTreeNoFollow's checks broken at once (each alone is covered by the other), and then the junction test fails.
- **Frontier D-001, the test integrity guard, 9 Nov:** a rule pack built into the engine (`packages/policy/src/test-integrity.ts`, beside the hidden-character check, so no rule or remembered rule lets it through) escalates a change that weakens the tests, at High risk (70) so Auto and Accept edits ask too, with a reason naming the weakening and the pattern in the rule id (`test-integrity-assertion-removed`, `-skip-added`, `-expected-changed`, `-test-deleted`, `-coverage-lowered`, `-failure-hidden`, `-shell-edit`). It reads Write (against the file as it is), Edit and MultiEdit to test files and test config, found by the path conventions of TS/JS, Python, C#, PowerShell Pester and Go plus the usual config files, and shell commands that delete or edit test files or hide a failure. The Host records `test_integrity_escalated` with the pattern before deciding, and refuses the call if the record fails. In Extreme approval it follows a setting per project (Settings, Sessions, Test integrity; `testIntegrity` in Gatehouse settings, by folder name or every project): by default the call is approved and flagged (a transcript line, a count in the session's state and its Details panel, where done-claims are said to be unverified; the evidence pack and done-claim verifier, D-003 and D-009, read the same records when they are built), and a strict project waits for a person. Locked tests (D-007) are not built yet. Golden: 15-test-integrity, 41 cases (21 weakening, 20 legitimate) across TS/JS, Python, C# and Pester. Threat model T76. Tests: test-integrity.test.ts (recorded before deciding, a person outside Extreme, approve and flag in Extreme, strict waits, refused unrecorded, strict by name). Break checks: the assertion count removed fails five named cases (#1, #6, #12, #17, #20: TS, JS Write, Python, C#, Pester); skip markers not compared, expected values not compared, the engine not asking the guard, strict ignored in Extreme and an unrecorded weakening each fail their tests. Found while running the cases: the shell splits a command at || before the guard saw it, so npm test || true slipped through; the guard now reads the whole command.
- **Frontier C-004, the memory governor, 9 Nov:** above a commit charge set in Settings, Sessions, Memory (85% unless chosen, 50 to 99 or off; `memoryGovernor` in Gatehouse settings, read live), Codehouse starts no new session or chat (`SessionManager.start`, any account), no session started again after a suspension, and no page look (`MemoryGovernor.admit`, `memory-governor.ts`). The reading comes from a new command of Codehouse-Helper.exe, `--memory` (GlobalMemoryStatusEx: the commit charge and its limit, and physical memory), so no PowerShell and no counter names that change with the language; it is kept for five seconds. A refusal is written to the audit log first (`memory_refused`, rule memory-governor, with the reading and the point) and is still given if the record fails, since refusing is the safe side; a reading the helper cannot give refuses nothing. The start screens show a card with how far memory is committed and the idle sessions (open, idle, nothing in the background or waiting), each with Suspend (`suspend_session`, recorded before it applies): its Claude Code ends, it stays in the sidebar marked suspended, and Start again brings it back on the same conversation when memory allows. Sessions coming back after a restart or a force stop are not refused: they were open before. This laptop read 82% committed when this was built. Tests: memory-governor.test.ts (the point, the refusal and its record, off, no reading, an unrecordable refusal still refused, the helper's line; and on real sessions under simulated pressure: a new session refused and recorded then started when memory falls, a restored one let through, suspend and Start again only when memory allows, a working session not suspended).
- **Fix, Extreme approval asked about an rmdir, 9 Nov (owner, with a screenshot: `mkdir -p data/ae && rmdir "$TEMP/ae" 2>/dev/null; ls data/ae` waited for them in Extreme approval):** G20 counted `rmdir` as a delete, and `$TEMP` made its target unreadable, so the call was taken as one that cannot be undone, which Extreme approval never approves alone. In the Bash tool the shell's `rmdir` removes only empty folders and can lose no file, so with no options, or only `-p`, `-v` and `--ignore-fail-on-non-empty`, it now counts as deleting nothing (`effects.ts`, `EMPTY_ONLY_RMDIR`). PowerShell's `rmdir` (an alias of Remove-Item, which removes files and whole folders), any other option, and anything else in the same command are judged as before. Break checks: rmdir counted as a delete again, PowerShell's rmdir let through, any option let through; each fails its test.
- **Frontier C-013, grant lifetimes, 9 Nov:** a rule remembered from a request (or a suggestion) now lasts 30 days and allows only in the session's folder unless you choose otherwise in the Remember panel (1, 7, 30 or 90 days or until removed; this folder or any; no limit, once more, 10 or 100 uses). The limits live in `rules.local.meta.json` beside `rules.local.yaml`, so an older version reading the rules after an undone update sees them as before (and ignores the limits, which only an older version can do); the rules file itself is unchanged and still refuses a field it does not know (a field that might widen a rule, golden 13-remember). The engine skips a remembered rule that has expired or is outside its folder (resolved with realKey), so the call goes on to the escalate rules and asks. The Host counts uses once per tool call (`GrantUses`, `grant-uses.json`), in the floor hook for Auto and Accept edits and in canUseTool, and a used-up rule asks with its reason. Fail closed throughout: unreadable limits expire the rule, an unreadable counts file spends every limited rule. The phone and push channels still cannot remember (invariant 11); a remember-for from them is refused. Break checks: no expiry check, no folder check, no use count, each fails its test.
- **Fix, ntfy 429 while listening, 9 Nov (a user's report: "Listening failed: ntfy answered 429"):** where the server holds the live stream back, Codehouse asked for replies every 3 seconds, and ntfy's default limit (a burst of 60 requests, then one every 5 seconds, sends included) ran out after about seven minutes. It now asks every 10 seconds; a 429 is not shown as a failure: Codehouse waits as long as Retry-After says (5 seconds to 15 minutes, a minute without it), asks half as often after it (up to once a minute), and the panel says when it listens again. A new loop after saving the settings keeps to the same wait, so saving again does not make it worse. Sending and the test say plainly that the server is limiting how often this computer may send. Break checks: no wait after a save, asking every 3 seconds, a 429 taken as a failure, each fails its test.
- **Frontier C-001, memory-aware test sharding, 9 Nov:** the whole suite now runs one test file per process, as many at once as free memory allows (`scripts/test-shards.mjs`, `npm run test:shards`): before each file starts, free memory less 2 GB kept back, at 1.5 GB a file, caps how many run together (at least one, never more than the cores less one). A file that fails runs once more and the retry is logged; one that passes only on its retry is listed as flaky; one that fails twice fails the run, with its output. The gate's full run uses it. Tests prove the cap under plenty and under pressure, the one retry, and that an injected failing file fails the run. Owed: the prompt's three full runs under 70% synthetic memory load, which take several hours on this laptop; the first full run is the gate of this change. 10 Nov: the gate's related tests use it too. When a run of them fails and its report says which files did not pass, those files run again each in a process of its own by free memory (a file that fails twice still fails), rather than the whole set again in one worker, which on a laptop short of memory died the same way an hour later (twice, in the Wave 1 release: no test failed, workers died with 0x80000003). With no report (the workers never started), the set runs once more in one worker, as before. 11 Nov: with less than 4 GB of memory free when it starts, the gate runs every test file this way instead of the related ones on two workers (five releases in a row had those workers die part way); slower, and it gets through.
- **Frontier C-002, the release refuses --skip-tests, 9 Nov:** `npm run release -- --skip-tests` now stops before anything is built unless the owner's override for exactly that version is beside the release key (`%USERPROFILE%\.codehouse-release\skip-tests.json`): the version, a reason and an expiry of at most a day, signed with the release key (Ed25519) and checked against the key built into Codehouse (`scripts/skip-tests-override.ts`). Its use is written to Codehouse's audit log (`release_skip_tests`, through the same locked, hash-chained writer the Host uses, redacted) before the release goes on; if the record fails, so does the release. The owner makes an override with `npx tsx scripts/release.ts allow-skip-tests "<why>"`, which runs only at a console and asks for the version to be typed. Without the flag a release runs the gate as before. Break checks: an unsigned override, an override for another version, each fails its test.
- **Owner decision, 9 Nov (no IT):** "I am never going to ask IT." The items that waited on IT are to be built as connectors to services that already exist, self-hosted or online. Recorded here; how each is built is in the backlog triage and the frontier status file.
- **Backlog G27, undo outside git, 9 Nov:** an undo point is now taken before every shell command that would delete files or throw work away (G20's `effectsOf`), however it is allowed: your rules, Auto, Accept edits, Extreme approval or you (`CheckpointManager.beforeEffects`, from the PreToolUse hook, which every call reaches; the escalation's own checkpoint is skipped when the same call was just saved). For a folder that is in no git repository, the checkpoints live in a git store of Codehouse's own under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Gatehouse\undo\<hash>` (`useShadowCheckpoints`), reached through GIT_DIR and GIT_WORK_TREE, so nothing is written into the folder; the Changes pane and its Undo work there as in a repository. The store keeps bytes exactly (no line-ending changes), leaves out folders that are rebuilt (node_modules, dist and the like) and every link or junction (found by walking without following them, then excluded), refuses a drive root, your profile folder or above it, and Windows, and a folder of over 20,000 files; looking at a folder never makes a store. With this, Replace (G35) also works outside git. Break checks: no undo point before a delete, a junction followed out of the folder, a store for a whole drive, each fails its test.
- **Tests less tied to the machine's speed, 9 Nov:** the WebSocket test client in `server.test.ts` now waits up to two minutes for a reply rather than 40 s, so the checkpoint and undo tests, which run git, no longer fail when other work keeps the machine busy (they failed that way several times this week and passed alone); each test's own time limit still stops a real hang.
- **Sessions on the phone, live, 11 Nov (owner: "I want a way to be able actually see the sessions remotely and be able to send prompts and see the ongoing status of the session. This is the priority"):** the phone's Sessions now shows each session as it goes. In the list, each session has its state with the working ring, its mode and, while a turn runs, the desktop's live line (Running a shell command (npm test), 12 s; Claude is writing src\app.ts; Claude is thinking). Tapping one opens it on a screen of its own: how it stands and Answer the request when one waits, its conversation (what you said, what Claude said and each tool it used with its target, oldest first, kept scrolled to the end) and a message box with Send and Interrupt, both signed with Face ID as before. The Host keeps each open session's steps and what it is doing in memory from its stream (`PhoneLive`, `apps/host/src/phone-sessions.ts`), seeded once from its transcript, so it shares within 3 seconds of a change instead of 15 and reads no transcript again; tool results and tool input never enter it, only a tool's target, which is redacted with everything else. The activity reader moved from the desktop into `@gatehouse/shared/activity`, so the phone words it exactly as the desktop does. The phone fetches every 3 seconds while the screen is in front of you and not at all while it is hidden, and says so when the computer has not shared for 90 seconds (asleep, or Codehouse closed). The snapshot stays under 44 KB, so sealed it fits the Relay's 64 KB limit, a Relay from before this too: up to 8,000 characters of the conversation for a session that works, waits on you or is among the newest three, 1,200 for the rest, then the oldest steps off the longest until it fits. A phone app from before this still shows the excerpt. Tests: `apps/host/test/phone-sessions.test.ts` (a working session's activity and steps, redacted, the transcript read once, a subagent's steps left out; your message shown at once and once; the snapshot fits with the newest steps kept; a change during a share is shared after it) and `apps/phone/test/app.test.tsx` (the live line in the list, the conversation and status on the session's screen, and back).
- **Backlog G32 and O18, third part, 9 Nov (starting from the phone, B35's safe variant):** a routine can be marked Can be started from the phone (`phone` on the routine, read tolerantly and recorded on save). The phone's snapshot lists those routines by id and name only (redacted); Start sends a `start` envelope naming the routine and nothing else (no session, no words, no folder), signed and sealed as a message is, with the same nonce, life and passkey checks (`verify.ts` refuses a start carrying anything else). The Host refuses while the kill switch is on and for any routine not marked for the phone, records `phone_start` first, then starts it through the routine's own start (`routineSessionOptions`: its folder, Manual mode, Extreme approval and a worktree as set) and notes the run for Recent runs. So the phone never chooses where work happens or what it is told beyond what you set up on the desktop. Break checks: starting any routine, a start carrying words or a session, each fails its test. With this G32 is done; O18 is done (sessions on the phone, prompts and Interrupt, signed and recorded).
- **Backlog G32 and O18, second part, 9 Nov (a message and Interrupt from the phone):** in the phone's Sessions, an open session takes a message (up to 4,000 characters) and, while it works, Interrupt. Each is an envelope of its own type (`prompt`, `interrupt`) naming the session and holding the words, with a fresh nonce from the phone and a life of a minute, signed with the passkey over the whole envelope (Face ID) and sealed to the Host's key with its own additional data (`COMMAND_AAD`), as the kill switch is. The Host accepts it only when the signature verifies against the pinned passkey with user verification, the nonce is unused (spent before anything happens), it lives at most two minutes, names no request, and names a session (`verify.ts`); then it refuses while the kill switch is on or when the session is not open here (or is an evaluation), records `phone_prompt` or `phone_interrupt` first (the phone, and for a message only its length; not recorded, not sent), and only then sends it on (`PhoneService.#command`). A changed word or a changed session breaks the signature. Push channels get none of this (invariant 11 as amended 30 Oct). Break checks: a session that is not open, the kill switch, an envelope living too long, each fails its test.
- **Backlog G32 and O18, first part, 9 Nov (sessions on the phone, read only):** the phone app has Sessions: the open sessions (newest first, up to 12) with how each stands, the requests waiting in it, and the latest of what was said (`phone-sessions.ts`: `latestSaid` over Claude Code's record, 1,500 characters, redacted with your terms before it leaves; invariant 12). The Host seals the snapshot to each paired phone's own key with its own additional data (`SESSIONS_AAD`, so it cannot pass for a request or a push; invariant 9) and puts it on the Relay (`PUT /api/device/sessions`), a moment after a session changes (at most every 15 s) and once a minute, only while a phone is paired; the Relay keeps the latest per phone in memory only, drops it when the phone is revoked, and gives it only to that phone's signed-in session (`GET /api/phone/sessions`). Nothing on the screen can change a session; prompts, Interrupt and starting from templates are the next parts, each to be signed with the passkey (invariant 11 as amended 30 Oct). Found and fixed while building it: the Relay's sign-in exemption matched any path beginning `/api/phone/session`, so the new route would have been reachable without a phone session; it now matches the sign-in paths exactly. This also corrects the Phase 66 (R10) line that said the phone had transcripts and Interrupt: until now it had neither; it now has this read-only view. Break checks: the route outside the sign-in, the excerpt unredacted, the phone opening anything sealed to it as sessions, each fails its test.
- **Backlog G36, git panel, 9 Nov (decided as recorded above):** a Git panel beside the transcript (`components/GitPane.tsx`, `git-panel.ts`). History is git's own graph of all branches (`git log --graph`, up to 10,000 commits, in a virtual list). Blame shows who last changed each line (`git blame --line-porcelain`). Stage lists each change from git's diff, not staged and staged, and stages or unstages one change at a time (`git apply --cached` of that one hunk, `--reverse` to unstage): only the index changes, never a file. Conflicts lists files a merge left in conflict; Open shows one in the viewer, where G33's editor resolves it, and Mark resolved runs `git add` only once the Host finds no conflict markers in the file on disk. Staging and resolving are recorded first (`git_staged`, `git_unstaged`, `git_resolved`; not recorded, not done). Paths are checked (no options, no way out of the repository) and git runs without a shell. Nothing here commits, pushes, merges or rewrites history; those stay with Claude's calls, under the rules. Also removed three hover hints added this week (Ask for a review, a step's commits, blame), which the no-tooltip rule forbids. Break checks: resolving with markers left, a path out of the repository, staging though not recorded, each fails its test.
- **Backlog G33, editing in the window, 9 Nov (decided as recorded above):** a text file in the session's folder has Edit in the viewer, which opens it in an editable CodeMirror view (`CodeEditor`: undo, search, Ctrl+S; no new dependency, chosen over Monaco for the same reasons as Phase 101). Save (`/api/files/save`) writes only if the file is still as it was when opened (the read now carries a hash), never to a file the floor blocks for Read or Edit, never outside the folder (no files Claude wrote elsewhere) and never past 1 MB, recorded first (`file_saved`, where and how big, never the text; not recorded, not saved), and written atomically. When the file changed outside, nothing is written and your text stays in the editor to copy (a merge view is not built: the backlog's "asks to merge" is met by refusing and keeping both). Deviation: the 5 MB responsiveness target cannot be met for editing, since files over 1 MB are not read in full; viewing larger files is unchanged. Break checks: overwriting a file changed outside, saving a secrets file, a save without the opened version, each fails its test.
- **Backlog G35, search and replace across files, 9 Nov:** a Search panel beside the transcript (`components/SearchPane.tsx`) searches the session's files with git grep in its own process with a time limit (`file-search.ts`: `--untracked` in a repository, `--no-index --exclude-standard` outside one, so ignored files are skipped; literal or regular expression, case optional, up to 2,000 matches), and a result opens its file. Replace shows each line it would change, file by file (`/api/files/replace/preview`), leaving out files the floor blocks, outside the folder, over 1 MB or not text; Replace in these files (`/api/files/replace`) takes a checkpoint (secrets left out, so Undo in Changes puts the files back), records `files_replaced` (counts and the checkpoint, never the text) and only then writes, and only files whose contents still match what was shown. A folder not in git is refused, as it could not be undone. Break checks: overwriting a file changed since the preview, changing a blocked file, replacing though not recorded, each fails its test. The 100,000-file timing in the backlog row is not measured on this laptop; git grep is the fastest tool already here.
- **Backlog U4 and conflict C2, decided 9 Nov (owner's standing rule: choose the best option, safety first, and record it):** gestures in proportion to risk, on every surface that can allow. On the desktop card a High request (risk 70 to 84) has Allow and Ctrl+Enter working one second after it appears (F17's delay, so a request that appears under the cursor or the hands is not allowed by a click or keys meant for something else), and a Severe one (85 and over) asks you to type allow, which works with a keyboard, a mouse and a screen reader alike (a hold is hard for some people and needs an alternative anyway); Edit then allow asks the same. On the phone a Severe Allow is pressed and held for 1.5 s and let go, then Face ID; the answer is sent from the release itself, because Safari asks for a passkey only from a person's own gesture. Queue rows and the request above the prompt box already send Severe to the card (Read it first, 8 Nov), focus never lands on Allow, and push channels never offer Allow for Severe (invariant 8). A Severe request can never become a remember rule (`checkRemember` refuses it). F17's batching is left out: a batch would put several decisions behind one gesture, the opposite of this item. Break checks: Severe allowed without typing, High allowed at once, a short hold on the phone, a Severe remember rule, each fails its test.
- **Backlog G31, evidence walkthrough, 9 Nov:** Walkthrough... in a coding session's menu shows its plan with each step's commits (G17), the files it changed (its Changes), every test run with whether it passed (`testRuns`, the same reading of a test command as G17), and the decisions worth a look from the audit log for that session (`/api/audit?session=`): a person's, Extreme approval's, the judge's, and every call not allowed, oldest first, each with its record number and Open record, which opens that record on the Audit page (`auditOpen`, read by number). Copy gives it as Markdown (`walkthroughMarkdown`). It is per session rather than per task step, since a session is where a task's work and records meet; G6's artifacts are not needed for it, and G30's bundle exports the records themselves.
- **Backlog G24, task board, 9 Nov:** a Board page (palette, and the sidebar's page list) with cards in To do, Running, Review and Done (`board.ts`, kept in `board.json`, written atomically, read tolerantly, so it survives a restart). Moving a card into Running for the first time starts its session in its folder with its prompt through the normal start (every check a session you start goes through), recorded first (`board_card_started`; not recorded, not started and not moved); a card with a session keeps it. Cards move by dragging, or by keyboard: Space picks a card up, the arrow keys choose its column and place, Space drops it and Escape puts it back, each step said in a polite live region; nothing is sent until the drop, so a card passed through Running starts nothing, and focus follows the card as it is drawn in each column. Break checks: starting though not recorded, a second session for a card that has one, focus lost on changing column, each fails its test.
- **Backlog U2, one inbox, 9 Nov:** the Approvals page now also lists, below the requests, the sessions that need you (`lib/needsYou.ts`): one stopped by an error, a run of Extreme approval that stopped short of its queue (turn or spending limit, a failed turn, two turns with no work), and a turn that may be stuck (two minutes with nothing from Claude and nothing waiting), each with Open. It is worked out each time from the Host's own session list, so its count is the Host's, it survives a restart, and an item goes as soon as its session moves on; there is nothing to dismiss and so nothing to fall out of step. The phone already gets the same three as sealed notifications (O16), and requests stay answered once on either side. Deviation: the phone's list shows requests only; showing these items in it too needs the phone to see sessions, which is G32 (waiting on the owner).
- **Backlog G23, automations, 9 Nov:** schedules and triggers were already there (routines, B34). A routine can now run in a new worktree each time (`worktree` on the routine, through `routineSessionOptions`), so a nightly job never touches your checkout. Each run is noted for a review inbox (`runs` in routines.json, the latest 50, read tolerantly; an older version keeps the key as it is), shown in Settings as Recent runs (`/api/routines/runs`): how each run's session stands, the actions waiting for you in it, the files it changed, its cost, and Open. Deviation: a blocked action in a routine's session parks as any request does, for the broker's hard deadline (30 minutes) and then is denied, as invariant 1 requires; it is not held longer, since a request answered hours later would be answered without its context. With Extreme approval nothing waits but what cannot be undone (G20). G26 (a sandbox) is not built, so routines run with the same rules and floor as every session. Break checks: the worktree dropped, runs not kept on disk, the waiting count hidden, each fails its test.
- **Backlog G25, best of several runs, 9 Nov:** Start a session with Work in a new worktree has Runs (1 to 4); with more than one, a prompt box asks what every run is to do, and each run starts in its own worktree with that prompt, opened side by side (B22's comparison, now for code). The bar over each run shows what it changed (files, lines added and removed, from its Changes), its last test run (G17's reading), the calls that asked you or were stopped (from its decisions), and its cost. Keep this one asks first, then records the choice (`compare_chosen`, now also for runs each in a worktree, by session and branch; coding sessions not each in a worktree are refused), closes the other runs and removes their worktrees through the recorded Remove (their branches stay), and points to Merge back for the one kept. Break checks: keeping without asking, the losing worktrees left, comparing coding sessions outside worktrees, each fails its test.
- **Backlog G20, effects and undo on approvals, 9 Nov:** before a shell command waits for you, the Host works out what it would delete or throw away (`effects.ts`): the targets of rm, rmdir, unlink, del, erase, rd and Remove-Item (with PowerShell's -Path and cmd's /s), walked without following links (up to 5,000 files), and the work not committed that git reset --hard, git restore, git checkout -- and git clean would lose (git status and a dry-run clean, read-only, no shell, 3 s each). Whether git can bring deleted files back is asked of git (tracked, and unchanged since the last commit); anything it cannot tell (a variable or home folder in a path, a deep pattern, a PowerShell filter, git not answering, the working out failing) counts as cannot be undone. The result travels on the request as `effects` (new optional field on `EscalationRequest`, redacted like the rest, included in the phone payload and its hash only when present, so other requests hash as before; the phone already hashes all it receives) and shows as an Effects line on the desktop card and the phone: how many files, the first 20, and how to undo it or why it cannot be. Extreme approval no longer approves a call that cannot be undone, and the Host turns the judge's allow of one into a question (the judge is limited to the network rule anyway); your own rules still allow what they allow. This makes Extreme approval wait in one more case, in keeping with the owner's rule that failures and floor denies still wait (invariant 3), and is recorded here as the backlog's acceptance asks. Break checks: Extreme approving it, the judge allowing it, a failed working out counted as nothing, git not answering counted as restorable, each fails its test.
- **Backlog G16, review card, 8 Nov:** Ask for a review in the Changes pane puts a request in the prompt box (`REVIEW_PROMPT`): review the session's changes, change nothing, and give the findings as a JSON list in a `codehouse-review` block. The reply shows that block as a card (`components/ReviewCard.tsx`, drawn by the transcript's code block when the block reads as findings; anything else stays a plain code block): findings grouped by file with severity, line and detail; Fix this one puts a request for that finding only in the prompt box; Fix all does the same for those left; Dismiss sends `review_dismiss`, recorded first (`review_finding_dismissed`, rule `review-dismissed`; not recorded, the finding stays) and then kept dismissed in the window. The block is Claude's words, so it is read as data and drawn as text, capped at 100 findings (a threat test in `xss.test.tsx`). No new tool or rule: the findings come in the reply, and every fix is a prompt you send.
- **Backlog G13, diff review, 8 Nov:** in the Changes pane every change in a changed file (not only files with several) is listed with the decision that allowed the edit that made it: `lib/hunkOrigin.ts` finds the latest Edit, Write, MultiEdit or NotebookEdit on that file in the transcript whose text and the change's lines hold one another (a short text must match whole, so a one-letter change is not claimed by any edit containing the letter), and the decision kept for that call is shown in the transcript's own words; no match says the change did not come from an edit of Claude's (a command, or you). Each change takes a comment; comments on any number of changes across files go together into the prompt box as one message, each with the lines it is about (`lib/reviewComments.ts`), for you to read and send. Keep or reject each change was already there (Undo per change, B12), as was every changed file in one list. Break checks: matching short changes by containment, dropping the decision from a change, each fails its test.
- **Backlog G17, plans tied to the work, 8 Nov:** a step in the Progress card lists the commits made while it was in progress (`git commit` through Bash or PowerShell, the hash from git's own line), kept when Claude writes the list again whole, and a step ticked off while the session's last test run had failed (npm test or gate, vitest, jest, pytest, go, cargo, dotnet, mvn, gradle) says so, and keeps saying so. Deviation: Codehouse marks such a step rather than stopping it being ticked, because the list is Claude's own and refusing the update would stop Claude, not the step; and it writes no spec files into your repository by itself (Claude writes them when asked). The queue's end read only from a reply's last line was done with O11. Break checks: commits lost on rewriting the list, the mark cleared by a later pass, each fails `transcript.test.ts`.
- **Backlog G11, performance budget, 8 Nov:** written down in `docs/performance.md`: a fresh copy ready in 30 s, only on-screen messages drawn, at most 400 diff lines drawn until asked, and the Approver's limits. Each release now measures the start: `package-smoke.mjs` times the packaged copy to its address, says SLOW over budget (said, not failed, as a busy computer slows a start too) and adds a line to `dist-app\start-times.jsonl`. The two diffs that drew every line (the transcript's edit diff and the Changes pane's) now draw the first 400 and a button for the rest (`components/MoreLines.tsx`). Deviation: no headless Edge frame trace or memory check runs on each release, because on this laptop both change with whatever else is running; the doc says how to look by hand.
- **Backlog G14, merge back, 8 Nov:** each worktree with commits on its branch has Merge back… in Settings, Sessions, Worktrees (`WorktreesPanel.tsx`). It first shows what the branch brings into the checkout's branch (`/api/worktrees/merge-preview`, `worktree-merge.ts`: its commits, `git diff --stat` and the whole diff, cut short past 400 KB, from where the two parted), any changes in the worktree not committed (not part of the merge), and any in your checkout (Merge is refused until they are committed or stashed, so your own work is never mixed in). Merge (`POST /api/worktrees/merge`) is recorded first (`worktree_merged`, rule `worktree-merged`; not recorded, not merged) and then merges exactly what was shown: it refuses when the checkout has moved to another branch or the worktree's branch has new commits since you looked (a session may still be working there), and runs `git merge --no-ff` on that commit with no shell. A merge that meets a conflict is undone at once (`git merge --abort`) and says so, leaving the checkout as it was. The other parts of G14 were already built: a worktree per session (B10, Phase 30), pruning only after you confirm (Remove), and no junctions followed (`folderBytes`). Break checks: without the branch-tip check, without the abort, and merging before recording, each fails `worktree-merge.test.ts`.
- **Backlog G6 left out, 8 Nov:** React artifacts are not built. React 19 ships no single file to load in a sandboxed page, so a React artifact would need a JSX compiler and React bundled into Codehouse as new dependencies, for what sandboxed HTML artifacts (D3, with Markdown) already do: Claude can write the same page as plain HTML and script. Mermaid is left out for the same reason (it too needs a new dependency). Sandboxed HTML and Markdown previews stay as they are.
- **Backlog G15 and G18, 8 Nov:** G15: a session in a worktree told to fix its pull request's failing checks now tries at most three times (`PR_FIX_ATTEMPTS`), then stops and says what still fails and what it tried, and is told never to merge (merging is yours; `gh pr merge` would ask you and be recorded anyway, which is the approval record). G18: `/tell <code> <message>` in a session's prompt box sends the message to another open session, by its code (GT 407), as your prompt there, through a new WebSocket command `tell`; the Host refuses a session telling itself, and records `message_relayed` with where it came from and went and its length, never its words. Side questions about a session were already `/btw` and Ask about code; @ now means files, so the message to another session is a command.
- **Backlog G29, 8 Nov:** each remembered rule on the Policy page says how often it was used this year and when last (`/api/policy/remember/usage`: allows by `remember-*` rules in the past year's audit log, with each rule's `createdAt` from `rules.local.yaml` through `readRememberEntries`). One not used for 90 days, from its last use or from when it was added, is marked, and one button asks you again about all of those. Rules do not expire by themselves: a rule that vanished unasked would surprise you, and removing is already one click; the marking does the reminding. Approval rate and decision time were already on the Approver page (analytics).
- **Backlog U8, 8 Nov:** four layout presets in the palette (`lib/layouts.ts`): one session, two side by side, four at once (the session you are on first, then the newest other open ones, never an ended one), and focus on one session, which hides the sidebar through a new `codeh:rail` event that App obeys. They set only the panes and the sidebar, which are already kept, so a layout survives restarts and updates. Side-panel widths are left out: the panels already size to the window, and a drag handle beside each would be the only new thing to keep.
- **Backlog O16, 8 Nov:** possibly stuck is now worked out on the Host as well as in the window (`stuck-watch.ts`): while a turn runs, nothing from Claude for two minutes with nothing waiting for you gives one `stuck` session event per turn (the time waiting for you does not count). That, and the end of a run of Extreme approval (`carry_on_end`: turn limit, spending limit, stalled, a failed turn, goal met; not a queue emptied, which is told as that, nor your own stop), now reach the paired phone (sealed, as every note) and ntfy, which gained a plain note with no buttons, its title and words redacted (`NtfyChannel.note`, invariant 9 as amended), and "build queue empty" goes to ntfy too (`noteFor` in `carry-on.ts`).
- **Backlog U16, Mica, 8 Nov:** the main window asks Windows for the Mica backdrop (`DWMWA_SYSTEMBACKDROP_TYPE`, main window) when its handle is made (`packaging/Backdrop.cs`), only on Windows 11 22H2 (build 22621) or later, with Transparency effects on (`HKCU\...\Themes\Personalize\EnableTransparency`, unreadable counting as off) and high contrast off; otherwise nothing changes and the window stays opaque, so there is no white flash and no Settings switch is needed (Windows' own Transparency effects is the switch). The page fills the window with its own backgrounds, so in practice Mica tints the title bar. The decision is built with Windows' compiler and run for real by `backdrop-exe.test.ts`; the whole window was compiled with it before commit.
- **Backlog F2 and F15, 8 Nov:** F2: the shipped rules that stop calls most often now say what to do instead, in their existing `message` (no new field, since the rules schema refuses unknown ones and an older version must still read a file): `destructive-shell` (force-with-lease, stash or a branch, `git clean -n`, named files), `outside-workspace-write` (inside the project, or ask for the folder to be added), `critical-paths` and `obfuscated-shell` (write it out, or a script file). The card's why-line and the deny Claude sees both carry it, so Claude can take the safer way by itself. F15: typing @ in the prompt box lists files in the session's folder that match (`/api/files/find`: a breadth-first walk that skips version control, dependency and build folders, never follows a link or junction, stops after 20,000 entries and gives the best 20, a name starting with what you typed first); arrows and Enter or Tab choose, Esc closes. A range after a mention, `@src/a.ts:10-20` or `@b.ts:7`, is written out when sent as "@src/a.ts (lines 10 to 20)", since Claude Code documents no range syntax (`lib/mentions.ts`).
- **Fix, a user's report, 8 Nov: ntfy "Listening failed: This operation was aborted" and never listening** (a self-hosted ntfy with a user name and password; test notifications arrived, answers were never heard, the sidebar said not connected). Two faults in `push/ntfy.ts`. First, saving the settings stops and starts the channel at once; the old listening loop caught its own abort, found `#running` true again (the new start), recorded the abort as a failure and carried on beside the new loop, each replacing the other's connection and status. Each loop now has a generation; only the current one may run, report or reconnect, and an older one ends silently. Second, and why it never listened: a server, or a proxy in front of it, that holds back streamed answers (nginx with buffering, some tunnels) never sends the live stream's first bytes, so the request just waited. If the stream has not begun within 15 seconds Codehouse now asks the server for replies every 3 seconds (`/json?poll=1&since=`, which such proxies pass at once), says so in the panel, and keeps going; a 401 or 403 on the reply topic now says the user must be allowed to read it. Tests for both (the save race, and a stream that never begins), each shown to fail with its fix taken out. Its gate failed only on `server.test.ts`'s checkpoint and undo tests, which take 65 to 96 seconds alone on this laptop against a 40-second reply limit; at the time the processor was at 100% with other projects' test runs (tripboard, family-time), and the same checkpoint test failed the same way on 6 Nov. Making those two tests less dependent on the laptop's speed is queued.
- **Backlog G21, characters you cannot see, 8 Nov:** a built-in escalation, `hidden-unicode` (risk 80), in the policy engine (`packages/policy/src/hidden.ts`): a command, path or pattern holding any invisible or direction-changing character (direction overrides and isolates, zero-width characters, word joiner, invisible operators, soft hyphen, a byte order mark, tag characters), or written text (Write content, Edit and MultiEdit new text) holding the two kinds used to hide meaning (direction overrides and isolates, the Trojan Source trick; tag characters, invisible ASCII), goes to a person whatever rule would allow it; a remember rule cannot lift it (`STRUCTURAL`). A joiner inside an emoji in written text is ordinary and is not counted. The approval card and the workspace's waiting request draw each such character as `[U+202E]` in place, with a note; Copy keeps the text as it is (`ShowHidden.tsx`). Goldens in `14-hidden-unicode.yaml`. **Found while building, a standing trap:** the editing tools wrote some `\u` escapes in new code as the raw invisible characters themselves; caught and put back, and the prohibited-pattern check (`scripts/forbidden.mjs`, run by the gate and every release) now refuses any raw direction, zero-width, word-joiner or tag character, or a byte order mark other than at a file's start, anywhere in the tree. It found three raw byte order marks in the claude.ai importer's own BOM handling, now written `﻿` (the same to JavaScript). Threat model T75.
- **Backlog triage and the first small items, 8 Nov:** every backlog row was checked against this report and the code (the result heads `docs/plan/backlog.md`). U6: the tool rows are already disclosure buttons with `aria-expanded` and arrow keys; a tree role would be wrong, as they sit between messages, so it is recorded as done with no change. O13: a preview address now belongs to one session and its folder and ends when that session closes or works elsewhere (`files-api.ts`); the local browser frame keeps `allow-same-origin`, which a dev server on another port needs and which cannot reach Codehouse's own origin. F22: the terminal's **Ask Claude about this** puts the selected output, or the last command and what it printed, into the session's prompt box as a fenced quote (`lib/compose.ts`, shared with Edit); nothing is sent by itself.
- **Backlog G30 and D9, an audit export that can be checked, 8 Nov:** the Audit page's Export now saves `codehouse-audit-<date>.zip` from `/api/audit/bundle` (`audit-bundle.ts`): the month files as they are (a last line still being written is left out), a `head.json` made from the last record copied (so a record written meanwhile cannot make it disagree), `VERIFY.txt` saying how to check it, and `verify-audit.ps1`. Both verifiers accept it, and one changed byte fails them. It is made in a folder of its own under the temp folder and removed straight after, and each export is recorded (`audit_exported`, its size and record count). It is not signed: the hashes are not keyed, as `docs/audit-format.md` says. `/api/audit/export`, the plain joined JSONL, stays for the Host's own use and its tests. Found while testing: the PowerShell verifier needed PowerShell 7.5 (`ConvertFrom-Json -AsHashtable -DateKind`, `Convert.ToHexString`, `SHA256.HashData`); under Windows PowerShell 5.1 it reported every record as not valid JSON, on the live log too. Fixed the same day (owner, 4 Nov: no PowerShell version is a hard dependency): in 5.1 it reads JSON with .NET Framework's own `JavaScriptSerializer` (which never turns a string into a date) and hashes with `SHA256.Create()`; the canonical form and checks are unchanged. `verify-ps.test.ts` now compares the Node verifier with both shells on every fixture, tampering included, and a break check makes the 5.1 run fail with the 7.5-only reader.
- **Backlog G19, the loop guard, 8 Nov:** the same call failing the same way three times in a row, with nothing changed in between, stops the turn on the Host (`loop-guard.ts`, from PostToolUse and PostToolUseFailure through `onToolOutcome`). Failures are compared by tool, command (or input) and error with digits and spacing smoothed, so timings do not hide a repeat. A fix cycle is not a loop: a successful Write, Edit, MultiEdit or NotebookEdit, a different failure, or the same call passing starts the count again; reads in between do not. The stop is recorded (`loop_stopped`, rule `loop-guard`), stops Extreme carrying on, and the screen says "Codehouse stopped this turn: the same command failed the same way three times in a row..." (a fourth stop reason, `loop`). The backlog's "pause on three failures" would have stopped ordinary test-and-fix cycles, so only the unchanged repeat counts; its budget alert is the spending cap and Extreme's limits already. The fake SDK gained failing tool calls for its test. Threat model T74.
- **Backlog U4, first step, 8 Nov:** a Severe request (risk 85 and over) can no longer be allowed from a row of the approvals queue, where its command is not shown: the row offers Deny and **Read it first**, which opens the full card (`QuickDecision` row variant). Below Severe the row keeps one-click Allow once; the card and the workspace keep Ctrl+Enter or a click inside them. The rest of U4 (a hold or typed confirmation for Severe on every surface, the phone included) is tied to conflict C2 in the backlog, so it waits for the owner.
- **Backlog G22, secrets in a commit, 8 Nov:** before a session's shell command that makes a commit runs, the floor hook reads what the commit would add (`git diff --cached`; every change since HEAD for `-a` or `--all`, or when the same command also runs `git add`, which has not run yet, plus every new file git would pick up, up to 1 MB each) and refuses it when an added line holds a private key or an Anthropic, OpenAI, GitHub, Slack, AWS or Google key, or a password in a web address (`commit-scan.ts`; strict patterns only, so ordinary code and test words are not stopped). The refusal is a floor deny, recorded as one, rule `secret-in-commit`, naming each kind and file and never the value. When it cannot read the commit (not a repository, git failing) the hook asks, so a person decides; it never allows (invariant 2). A plain in-repo scanner, as the owner's rule asks (never gitleaks). Your own commits in your own terminal are yours and are not scanned. Its tests build their fake keys at run time, so the test file holds none. Threat model T72.
- **Backlog O11 and O10, 8 Nov:** QUEUE EMPTY and GOAL MET now count only as the last line with words in a reply (markdown such as bold or a full stop around it allowed), so a reply that quotes the instruction or says it will end with it no longer ends Extreme's run or a goal (`endsWithLine` in `carry-on.ts`). The progress card and Plan panel now follow Claude Code's task tools as well as TodoWrite: TaskCreate adds a task (its id taken from the answer's structured result, or "Task #n created" in its text), TaskUpdate changes its status, words or "doing" line by id, or removes it when deleted; an update for an unknown id changes nothing (`applyTaskCall` in `lib/transcript.ts`). Subagents' task calls are left out, as their own lists.
- **Dependency, 6 Nov: `@fastify/static` 10.1.7 to 10.1.10** (apps/host and apps/relay, pinned exactly). Why: `npm audit` reported 11 moderate advisories; the two that reach the shipped app came through it, `glob` 13.0.6 (GHSA-3g3m-2fjr-6pw4) and `minimatch` 10.2.6 (GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249). Low risk here (its patterns are fixed in code, and it serves only on 127.0.0.1 behind the token), but 10.1.10 is a patch release that moves to `glob` 14.0.4 and `minimatch` 11.0.5, so the app ships neither. The 9 left are `minimatch` 10.2.6 inside ESLint and typescript-eslint, development tools that are not shipped; their only fix is an ESLint downgrade, so they stay until ESLint moves. No new package.
- **Left out, 6 Nov (standing decision: the best option, safety first):** computer use (the SDK has no computer-use tool; Codehouse driving the real mouse and keyboard by screen position is beyond what any rule can check, and whole-screen pictures cannot be redacted; browser tools cover checking your own pages), a chat's outputs folder (superseded: Ask about code chats change nothing), and B30, a local codebase index (Claude Code's Grep and Glob already search with its own ripgrep; an index would add a dependency and a second copy of the code to protect).
- **Ask about code (owner, 6 Nov: "Since we cant really sync chat with claude.ai I want to move this feature to something more useful... a section where you can chat about an existing code session"):** the sidebar's Chat side is now **Ask about code** (New question, Questions), and its page **Ask about a code session**: a session must be chosen (open coding sessions only, one chosen by itself when it is the only one), and a code session's ⋯ menu has **Ask about this session...**, which opens the page with it chosen (`lib/askAbout.ts`, `newChatAbout`). `create_session` carries `about` (the code session's id); the manager refuses it for a coding session, for a session that is not open and for another chat, and keeps `about: { id, title, cwd, sdkSessionId }` on the chat (`SessionInfo.about`, kept through a restart in `open-sessions.json`, read tolerantly). Such a chat is a chat as before (Plan mode, `chat-no-shell`, no writes or commands, invariants unchanged); `composeInstructions` tells it the session, its folder, to read with Read, Glob and Grep and to change nothing, with how the session stood and the latest of what was said in it (`readAbout` in `about-session.ts`: Claude Code's own record through `SessionHistory.allMessages`, your words and Claude's only, the latest 30,000 characters, marked as a record, redacted). The Companion offers it `read_session`, which reads the same again at any time; the rule `session-read-tools` allows it for chats only, from the in-process Companion. A chat about a session never uses the warm chat. Chats kept from before stay as they are; projects, research and comparing stay on the page; the claude.ai import stays in Settings, Privacy, and the empty list no longer points to it. The Home screen's buttons are over its artwork, which says Chat, so they are unchanged. Threat model T71.
- **Settings overhaul, browser tools:** Settings, Sessions, Browser tools (`browserTools` in Gatehouse settings, off by default). On, a coding session started from then has the Companion tool `look_at_page({ url })`: a 1280 by 800 picture of a page on this computer and its text. `apps/host/src/page-look.ts` refuses anything but http or https on localhost or 127.0.0.1 (no name or password) before Edge starts, then runs Edge headless twice side by side with its documented `--screenshot` and `--dump-dom` switches, each in its own `--user-data-dir` made for the look (never your profile; no debugging, automation or extension switch, the switches the Chat build prompt forbids), redacts the text, returns the PNG as an MCP image block, and removes the profiles however the look ends (owner, 1 Nov: leaked Edge profiles filled the disk). A chat never has it: the tool is not registered, and the rule `page-look-tools` allows it only with `sessionKinds: [code]` and `mcpSource: [sdk]` (golden cases now carry a session kind, `sk`). Measured on this laptop: about 23 seconds a look, most of it Edge starting. It looks only; clicking and typing are not offered. Threat model T70.
- **Changelog in Settings (owner, 4 Nov: "every single changelog for every version... a drop down which shows version number and the release date"):** Settings, This computer, Changelog. Each release now attaches `changelog.json`, every published release's version, date and What is new notes (`whatIsNew` takes that part of each GitHub release page), made by `scripts/release.ts publish` through `gh api` and checked with `parseChangelog` before anything is published. The Host's `GitHubSource.changelog()` reads the newest one the way `update.json` is read (the public download path with no rate limit, or the API asset with a token), `ChangelogStore` keeps the last copy in `updates/changelog.json` and shows it offline, and reads again at most hourly unless asked (`GET /api/changelog?fresh=1`); a page that is not a changelog never replaces the kept copy. The panel opens at the installed version, lists each as "2.0.97, released 6 Nov 2026", and shows notes as text (headings, list items, lines), never HTML. GitHub holds releases from 2.0.1 (2 Nov); the ones before the rename were removed with it, so the list starts there. Releases before this one have no `changelog.json`, so the list appears from the next release on.
